CORAM : DIVAN J.

Oral Judgment. 
This is an elector s petition challenging the election of the respondent to the 
Lok Sabha from Godhra Parliamentary Constituency No. 16 . The election which 
is challenged was held at the time of the last general clections on February 18 , 
1967, and the result was declared on February 24 , 1967. At this clection for the 
Lok - Sabha seat the candidates were the first respondent, sponsored by the 
Swatantra Party , Maneklal Gandhi, sponsored by the Congress Party , Babubhai 
Amin , sponsored by the Praja - Socialist Party and one Bipin Bhatt , who contest 
ed the election as a non - Party vandidate . When the results of the elections 
were declared , it was found that the respondent won the election by a margin of 
nearly 24 ,449 votes over his nearest rival, Maneklal Gandhi; and 1,519 votes were 
rejected by the Returning Officer and the respondent having obtained the Highest 
number of votes in his favour was declared elected by the Returning Officer on 
February 24 , 1967. 


In this petition , the petitioner has prayed for a declaration that the election 
of the respondent to the Lok Sabha from Godhra Parliamentary Constituency is 
void and ineffective and for further and other reliefs and costs of the petition . 
The petitioner has allcged in his petition that different types of corrupt prac 
tices were committed by the respondent or by some persons or by persons other 
than the respondent with his consent and the corrupt practices which are alleged 
are, firstly , that in contravention of the provisions of s . 123 ( 7 ) of the Repre 
gentation of the People Act, 1951 (hereinafter referred to as the Act ) the reg 
pondent, his election agent and others hud obtained and procured the assistance 
of three Police Patels in order to further the election prospects of the respon 
dent. The second corrupt practice which has been alleged against the respon 
dent is that he had incurred or authorized expenditure in contravention of the 
provisions of S . 77 of the Act. The third corrupt practice which is alleged 
against the respondent is that the election agent of the respondent committed 
an offence of bribery by offering and paying a sum of Rs. 1 ,500 to one Jesinobhai 
Hemabhai Maliwad , who was a duly nominated candidate for the Gujarat State 
Legislative Assembly seat from Salia Constituency . The area of Salia Consti 
tuency is included in the larger area covered by Godhra Parliamentary Consti 
tuency ; and Jesingbhai Hemabhai Mallwad was the candidate sponsored by the 
Praja Solialist Party and within the time permitted by law he had withdrawn) 
his candidature ; and the allegation against the respondent is that contrary to 
the provisions of S . 123 ( 1 ) ( A ) ( a ) of the Act, gratification was offered and paid 
In order to induce the said Jesingbhai Hemabhai Maliwad not to stand as a candi. 
date at this election . It is also alleged against the respondent that he and his 
Agents and his workers utilized the pictorial representation of Gandhili in order 
to canvass votes and it is therefore , alleged that there was an appeal hv the 
respondent or his agents or by other persons with the consent of the respondent 
to vote for the Swatantra Party candidates by use of national symbol, viz . , pictorial 
representation of Gandhiji; and in this connection it has been contended on behalf 
of the petitioner that the pictorial l epresentation of Gandhiji is a national sy117 
bol or emblem and that surh use or appeal was made for the furtherence of the 
prospects of the election of the respondent and for prejudicially affecting the 
election of the candidate sponsorded by the Congress Party . 

In his Written Statement the respondent has denied the different allegations 
of facts and he has also raised certain technical contentions. For the purposes 
of this judgmet, I need only refer to the technical contention which has been 
relied upon even at the stage of arguments before me viz ., that because of the 
non - joinder of Jesinghhat Hemabhai Maliwad as 3 Party - Respondent in this 
petition , there is a fatal non - joinder so far as S . 86 read with S . 82 of the Act 
is concerned and , therefore , on the ground of non - joinder of Jesingblini Hemabhai 
Mallwad the petition is llable to be dismissed . The other technical ohjections do 
not require mention at this stage . 


dismissed.nondioinder copulence 82 of the this 


On the rival contentions of the parties , the following issues were raised : 

(1 ) Whether the petition is barred by limitation ? 
( 2 ) Whether the petition fails to comply with the provisions of Ss. 81, 32 

or 89 of the Representation of the People Act, 1951 ? If yes , whether 
the petition is maintainable ?

When the matter reached hearing before me on January 29 , 1968, Mr. Raval, 
on behalf of the respondent stated that he did not wish to press issues Nos . 1, 3 
and 4 ; and Mr. Daru , on behalf of the petitioner stated at that time that in view 
of the judgment delivered by me in Election Petition No. 24 of 1987, on October 
23, 1967, he did not wish to urge uny arguments or lead any evidence on issue 
No. 7 ; and , therefore, no evidence has been led nor have any arguments been 
advanced before me regarding issuc No. 7 . As regards issue No. 8 regardin : the 
incurring or authorizing of expenditure in contravention of the provisions of 
S . 77 of the Act, on January 30 , 1968, when the Court assembled for the day , 
Mr. Daru , on behalf of the petitioner , informed me that he did not wish to press 
issue No. 8 any longer and , therefore, he has not led any further evidence after 
that stage so far as issue No. 8 is concerned and no arguments have been advanc 
ed before me as regards issue No. 8 . As regards issue No. 9 , regarding the offer : 
of Rs. 1, 500 to Tesinhhai Hemabhai Maliwat and the payment of that sum of 
money to Jesingbhai Hemabhal Maliwad, some vidence was led on behall of the 
petitioner and two witnesses, viz , Mavsing K : labhai Parmar , Petitioner s Witness 
No. 14 ; and Jesingbhai Hemabhai Maliwad , Petitioner s Witness No. 20, were 
examined After leading the evidence of these two witnesses , though the issue 
has not been in terms given up , no arguments have been advanced on behalf of 
the petitioner regarding this particular allegation of bribery under S . 123 ( 1 ) of 
the Act ; and Mr. Daru has very wisely not addressed any arguments on that 
point. In view of the evidence of Jesingbhai Hemabhni Maliwad , who has clearly 
denied having been offered or paid any sum for withdrawing his candidature and 


Sec , 3 ( 1) ] THE GAZETUE OF INDI: JUNE 8 , 1968 / FYAIST HA 18 . 1890 2661 

= 
in view of the fact that Jcsingbhai has not been declared a hostile witne3s , it is 
clear that the petitioner s case regarding this allegation of bribery could never 
have been established . Mavsing s evidence in view of the contradictions brought 
on record also does not inspire any confidence and , thereforc , very wisely no 
arguments have been advanced before me regarding issue No. 9 . 


Under these circumstances, the only issues which survive for my considera 
tion at the present stage are issues Nos . 2 , 5 and 6 , rest of the issues having been 
either expressly given up or not pressed at the stage of arguments before Inc in 
view of the evidence already recorded in this case . So far as issues Nos, 2 and 
$ gre concerned , both these issues relate to the alleged defect in the petition on 
the ground of non - joinder of Jesingbhai Homabhai Maliwad , as a Party - Respon 
dent to this petition . It is to be borne in mind that the election which is challeng 
ed in the present proceedings is to the Lok - Sabha seat from Godhra Pariiamen 
tary Constituency . Jesingbhai Hemabhal Maliwad was not a candidate from this 
constituency. He was a candidate from Salia Constitucncy for the Gujarat State 
Legislative Assembly Seat. It is true that the area covered by Salia Assembly 
Constituency seat is included and is a part of the larger area covered by the 
Godhra Parliamentary Constituency and it is also true that at the time of the 
last general elections, the elections to the State Legislative Assembly and to the 
Parliament were held on one and the same day. Each clector had to cast his 
votes for the Parliamentary Constituency as well as for the State Legislative 
Assembly Seat on one and the same day but the elections were to different legis 
lative bodies and the constituencies were not identical. It is well-settled law 
that if there are any allegations of corrupt practice against any candidate , he 
raust be joined as a party to the petition and in view of the decision of the 
Supreme Court in K . Kamaraja Nadar v . Kunju Thevar. A . I. R . 1958 S . C ., 187 , it 
k clear that even a person who has withdrawn his candidature within the time 
termitted by law , is a candidate so far as the provisions of S . 82 (b ) of tlie Act 
are concerned , By S . 82 of the Act provision is made regarding the parties to the 
petition and S . 82 provides that a petitioner shall join as respondents to his peti 
tion all the contesting candidates other than the petitioncr ; and where a declara 
ton that the election of all or any of the candidates was void is sought all the 
returnci candidates are required to be joined as Party - Respondents under cl. ( a ) 
of s . 82 . Then cl. (b ) provides ; --- 
" A petitioner shall join as respondents to his petition any other candidate 

against whom allegations of any corrupt practice are made in the 
petition ." . 


Now , the words: Many other candidate " occurring in sub - section (b ) of S . 82 
clearly indicates that the candidates other than those who are referred to in sub 
section ( a ) and against whom allegations of any corrupt practice are made must 
be joined as party - respondents in every election petition . The provisions of S . 82 
( b ) are not to be read in isolation and they must be read in the context of sub 
section ( a ) of that sertion and when the two sub - sections are read together , it 
is clear that the candidates who are required to be joined must be candidates 
either contesting or non - contesting , who were candidates for the election . which 
is sought to be challenged . Under $ . 79 ( b ) , a candidate means a person who 
has been or claims to be dulv nominated as a candidate at any election and any 
such person shall be deemed to have been a candidate as from the time when , 
with the election in prospect, he began to hold himself out as a prospective 
candidate . It is, therefore . clear that though Jesingbhai Hemabhai Maliwad was 
a candidate within the meaning of S . 79 ( b ) of the Act. he was a candidate for 
the Gujarat State Legislative Assembly Seat from Salia Constituency and he was 
not a candidate for the Lok Sabha Seat from Godhra Parliamentary Constituency , 
Under these circumstances , he cannot be said to be a candidate 50 far as the 
respondent, who is a returned candidate from Godhra Parliamentary Consti 
tuency is concerned . The election which is challenged and the declaration in 
respect of which is sought for in this petition is the election to the Goddhra 
Parliamentary Constituency and not the election from Salja Lepislative Assembly 
Constituency . Under those circumstances, it is clear that Jesingbhai Hemabhai 
Maliwad , against whom there are allegations of having agreed to receive the sum 
of Rs. 1 , 500 for withdrawal of his candidature and further of having received the 
Dayment of that sum as a consideration for withdrawal of his candidature ( and 
those are allegations of corrupt practice under S . 123 ( 1 ) ( B ) ( a ) was not a 
necessary party to this petition . As a matter of fact he was not at all a proper 
party to this petition , the two Constituencies being entirely different. Hence 
these two issues Nos , 2 and 5 must be answered against the respondent. I must

say at this stage in fairness to Mr. Nanavati, the learned counsel for the respon 
dent, that though he has not given up these points , he has not seriously canvases 
these points before me. 

That leaves now only the question as regards issue No. 6 ; and that is regarding 
the use of the services of the three Police Patels for the election canpaign of 
the respondent. 

In this connection , it is alleged that the respondent or his election agent or 
some person named in the petition with the consent of the respondent or his elec 
tion agent obtained or procured the assistance from Punabhai Pratapsing Patel , 
Kalubhal Haribhai and Shanaji Punajaji; and that this was done for the further 
ance of the prospects of the respondent s election , 

It is not in dispute belore me that at the relevant tine Punabhai Pratapsing 
Patel of Chandpur village in Godhra Taluka , Kalubhai Haribhai of Parabla village 
also in Godhra Taluka and Shanaji Punjaji oť Mava village in Malpur Taluka of 
Sabarkantha Dlatrict, were the Police Patels of their respective villages. Evidence 
has been brought on the record in the shape of Exs. " A " and " F " , to show that 
Purabhai Pratapsing and Kalubhai Haribhal were appointed Police Patelsoľ 
Chandpur and Parabia villages respectively and Shanaji Punaji had been appoint 
ed Police Patel of Nava village . It is also not in dispute before me that Punabhai 
Pratapsing acted as the Polling Agent of the respondent at Chandpur Polling 
Station . It is also not in dispute before me that Kalubhai Haribhai acted as the 
polling Agent of the respondent at Morwa- Hadap village, where there was a Polling 
Station at the Guparati Primary School located in that village, and it is also not 
in dispute before me that Shanaji Punjaji Damor acted as the Polling Agent of 
the respondent at Masadra village Polling Station in Sabarkantha District . It is 
common ground between the parties that several villages of Banaskantha District 
were included in the area covered by Godhra Parliamentary Constituency and 
villages of Nava , and Masadre were included in the area of Godhra Parliamentary 
Constituency . Exhibits " H " and " I" on the record of this case , are the forms 
appointing Kalubhai Haribhal as the Polling Agent of the respondent at Morwa 
Hadap Gujarati School Polling Station and Punabhai Pratapsing as the Polling 
Agent of the respondent at Chandpur Polling Station respectively ; Ex . " M " is the 
forn appointing Shanaji Punjajt Dumor as the Polling Agent of the respondent at 
Masadra ; and each of these three forms, Exs. " H " , " I" and " M " bear the rubber 
stamp impression of the signature of the respondent. 


The questions that arise for my consideration in connection with issue No . 6 
are partly questions of law and partly questions of fact. The first question that I 
have to consider in this connection is whether a Police Patel functioning under the 
Bombay Village Police Act , 1867 , is a member of the Police Forces within the 
meaning of S . 123 ( 7 ) ( d ) of the Act ; secondly , whether a police patel acting as a 
polling agent at a polling station , which is situated within the area of his jurisdic 
tion can be said to further the election prospects of a candidate for whom he le 
acting as a polling agent and whether the candidate so appointing him as his 
polling agent can be said to obtain or procure the assistance of a Government 
servant within the categories set out in S . 123 ( 7 ) of the Act for the purpose of the 
prospects of his own election ; and thirdly , whether there was any procuring or 
obtaining of the assistance of Punabhai Pratapsing, Police Patel of Chandpur, 
Kalubhai Haribhai, Police Patel of Parabia and Shanaji Punjaji Damor , Police 
Patel of Nava village , for the furtherance of the election prospects of the respon 
dent. 


It is clear that out of these three questions unless and until the first question is 
derided in favour of the petitioner , questions Nos. 2 and 3 are not required to be 
answered , if it is held that a Police Patel functioning under the provisions of the 
Bombay village Police Act of 1867 , is not a member of the Police Forces, other two 
questions regarding Issue No. 6 are not required to he answered at all and there 
fore. I will take up question No. 1 Arst . 


The words of S . 123 ( 7) (d ) of the Act are "members of the Police forces ; and 
it is useful in this connection to also refer to the provisions of cl. ( f ) of s . 123 ( 7 ) of 
the Act, which refers to revenue omcers other than yillage revenue officers known 
Als lambardars, malguzars , Patels, deshmukhs or by any other name, whose duty 
j: ) collect land revenue and who are remunerated by a share of, or commission 
on the amount of land revenue collected by them but who do not discharge any 
nolice functions. T may mention that by the Explanation to s . 123 , it has been 
provided that for the purposes of cl. ( 7 ) of s. 123 of the Act a person shall be de 
emed to assist in the furtherance of the prospects of a candidate s election 11 


Sec. 9 (ii)] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1968 / JYAISTHA 18. 1890 


2663 


he acts as an election agent of that candidate . Prior to the 
amendment by s . 53 of the Representation of the People (Amendment) Act , 1986, 
after the words " election agent" , the words " or a polling agent or a counting 
agent" were to be found in cl. ( 2 ), of Explanation to S . 123 ( 7 ) of the Act ; but as 
a result of this amendment, the words " or a polling agent or a counting agent 
have been omlited . The result, therefore, is that prior to the amendment of 1966 , 
a person acting as a Polling Agent of a candidate was to be deemed to assist in 
the furtherance af the prospects of that candidate s election under s . 123 ( 7) of 
the Act; but after the amendment this deeming clause no longer applies to a 
polling agent. 


At this stage, it is necessary to set out a bit of Legislative history . So far as 
the Bombay Presidency was concerned , the first legal provision regarding police 
legislation in Bombay and Sind was Bombay Regulation No XII 
of 1827. It was a regulation for the establishment of a 
system of police throughout the zillas subordinate to Bombay for providing rules 
for its administration and for defining the duties and powers of al police autho 
ritles and servants. Bombay Regulation XII of 1827 , governed all police establish 
ments whether of village police or of police in districts or urban areas in any 
part of Zillas subordinate to the Presidency of Bombay. Thereafter the Central 
Legislature passed the police Act, being India Act V of 1861. Under S . 46 of that 
Act it was provided that Act was not to take effect by its own operation in any 
Presidency , state or place but the State Government by an order to be published 
in the official gazette should extend the whole or any part of that Act to any 
Presidency , State or place. No such Notification or order was issued by the Gov 
ernment or Bombay and India Act V of 1861 does not appear to have been brought 
into force in the Presidency of Bombay. However, in Bombay Presidency in 1867 
two enactments were paşsed ; one was the Bombay District Police Act being Bom 
bay Act No. 7 of 1867 ; and as pointed out by K . S . Gupte in his commentary on 
the Bombay District Police Act , at page 10 of 1942 Edition , this Act ( Bombay 
District police Act VII of 1867 ) was nothing more than an application of the Gov 
ernment of India Police Act ( V of 1861) to the Presidency with such modifications 
as the local circumstances required . In the very same year, the Bombay Legisla 
ture also passed Bombay village Panchayat Act ( Bom . Act VIII of 1867 ) ; and thus 
a distinction was made from 1887 onwards between village police and District 
Police . The Bombay District Police Act of 1867 was repealed and replaced by 
Bombay District Police Act (Bombay Act IV . of 1890 ) in 1902 , the City of Bombay 
Police Act 1902 was passed for the town and island of Bombay . Thereafter in 
1951, the Bombay Police Act , 1951, was enacted in order to consolidate and amend 
The law for the regulation of the Police Force in the State of Bombay . The pream 
ble to the Bombay Police Act , 1961 states that particular statute was being enacted 
in order to amalgamate the District Police Forces and Greater Bombay . Police 
Forces ; and after 1956 , the Police Forces of Saurashtra , Kutch , Hyderabad and 
Vidarbha Regions were to be common Forces and to introduce uniform methods 
regarding the working and control of the said Forces throughout the State and 
by the Bombay Police Act of 1951 the Bombay District Police Act, 1890 and the 
City of Bombay Police Act, 1902 - were repealed because Instead of the separate 
police forces governed by those two enactments, one common . police force which 
was to be governed by the Bombay Police Act, 1951, was proposed to be set up . 
By S . 3 of the Bombay Police Act 1951, A s adopted and applied to the State of 
Gujarat, it has been provided that there shall be one police force as a whole in the 
State of Gujarat, section 168 of the Bombay Police Act , 1951, provides as 
Follows: 
" Nothing in this Act Shall affect the provisions of the Bombay Village Police 

Act, 1867 . .. 
· or any enactment which may be made in regard to the reserve Police . 

Therefore though by S , 3 , provision is made that there shall be one Police Force 
for the whole of the State of, Gujarat , the operation of the provisions of Bombay 
Village Police Act, 1867, or any enactment which may be made in regard to the 
reserve Police has been saved by S . 16 of the Bombay Police Act 1951 . 


Mr. Nanavati. . on behalf of the respondent, has contended before me that when 
the Parliament used the words; " members of the Police forces" in S . 123 ( 7 ) ( d ) of 
the Act, it was using a well -known terms of legislative parlance ; and according to 
this contention , the words " police forces " in cl. ( d ) refer only to such forces as are 
set up by a special legislative enactment in that behalt by any competent legisla 
tive authority . He has rightly pointed out that both under the Government 
of India Act , 1935 and also under the Constitution , " Police " is a subject in the 


2664 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JENE X, 1968 / JYAISTHA 18 . 1890 


PART II 


AO 


Provincial List so far as Government of India Act, 1935 , was concerned , and in the 
State List so far as the Constitution is concerned ; and the contention to that unless 
& force has in fact been set up by a legislative enactment providing for the consti 
tution , organization and discipline of a police force , it cannot be said to be a police 
force for the purposes of cl, ( d ) of S . 123 ( 7 ) of the Act . In this connection Mr. 
Nanavati has drawn my attention to the fact that by S . 2 of the Police Act, 1861 
( India Act V of 1861) , the entire police establishment under the State Government 
for the purposes of that Act is to be deemed one police force and is to be formally 
enrolled and consists of such number of officers and men and has to be constituted 
to such manner as shall from time to time be ordered by the State Government; 
nd under the definition S . 1 , the word " police " includes all persons who shall be 
inrolled under the Act V of 1861) . Under S . 4 of the Bombay District Police Act 
V90 , it was provided as follows : 
" In each district of the Presidency of Bombay to which this Aot extends , 

the Provincial Government may establish and entertain a Police force 
of such number in the several ranks and having such an organization 
and such duties , rights and authority as are hereinafter prescribed 

and provided for." 
In the Bombay Police Act, 1951 by S . 3 , as pointed out earlier , it has been provided 
that there shall be one police force for the whole of the State of Gujarat; and 
provision is made by S . 5 for the Constitution of that police force and there arc also 
provisions for organization and discipline of that police force under the Bombay 
Act, 1951. 

The Bombay Legislature also passed another Act called the Bombay State 
Reserve Police Force Act, 1961 (Bom . Act XXXVIII of 1951) and S . 3 of that Act 
provided that in addition to the Police Forces constituted under the Bombay 
Police Act, 1951, the State Government may establish and maintain an arthed 
reserve police force known as the State Reserve Police, Force ; and the State 
Reserve Police Force has to be established and maintained in such manner as 
may be prescribed . Under Bom , Act XXXVIII of 1961, provision is also made 
for the organization of the State Reserve Police Force into groups, division , bet 
talions and platoons. There is also provision made in this Act for the State 
Reserve Police Force regarding the appointment of officers and maintenance of 
discipline of this Reserve Police Force . 

Mr. Nanavati, on behalf of the respondent, has also drawn my attention to 
the fact that for the better protection and security of the railway properties, 
India Parliament has passed an Act called Railway Protection Act , 1957, which 
ig an Act to provide for the constitution and maintenance of a force called the 
Railway Protection Force ; and S . 3 of that Act provides that there shall be con 
stituted and maintained by the Central Government a force to be called the Rail 
way Protection Force for the better protection and security of the railway pro 
perties . It is clear that protection and security of property is one of the police 
functions and this Railway Protection Force has been set up under the provisions 
of the Railway protection Force Act, 1967, to discharge those police functions , so 
tar as railway property is concerned . In this case also by a legislative enactment 
a special force has been constituted and provision has been made for the organiza 
Hon and discipline of that special force under the Rallway Protection Force Act, 
1967. 


12th February , 1968 . 
On the basis of these different enactments, Mr. Nanavati urged before me that 
the words : " members of the police forces " are well - recognised in legislative 
parlance and, therefore, when the Legislature uses the words "members of the 
Police forces in S , 123 ( 7 ) (d ) of the Act, it must be deemed to refer to a police 
force constituted and organized by a special enactment of the Legislature which 
in terms constitutes such a police force and for which order and discipline are 
provided for by such a statute. He has contended that in the Bombay Village 
Police Act , 1867 , there are no words constituting a police force known as Bombay 
Village Police Force but it merely provides for discharge of certain duties by 
the Police Patels appointed under the Act. This , if one may say so , 18 a narrower 
Inter - pretation which is sought to be placed on the words "members of the police 
forces " . On the other hand , the wider interpretation which has been contended 
for by Mr. Daru , on behalf of the petitioner is that the Court should consider 
the aspect from the point of view of the duties which the Police Patels are 
called upon to discharge and also from the point of view of the functions which 
guch Police Patels perform . In my opinion , the wider view , which Mr. Daru 


SEC , 3 ( 1)] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8 , 1968 /JYAISTHA IS . 1990 


2665 


has contended for , should be placed upon the words: " members of the police 
forces" occuring in $ . 123 ( 7 ) ( d ) of the Act. In the frst place, the Legislature 
in enacting S . 123 ( 7 ) has taken care to see that officers of the Government, some 
of whom may be even in a low position like the Jawan serving in one of the 
armed forces of the Union should not be called upon to render assistance for 
furtherance of the prospects of any candidate s election and if such assistance 
has been obtained or procured contrary to the provisions of $ . 123 ( 7 ) , that it 
amounts to a corrupt practice . Clause ( f ) of S . 123 ( 7 ) also comes in aid for 
interpretation of cl. ( d ) . Under cl. ( f ) , assistance under the circumstances set 
out in the main body of S , 123 ( 7 ) of revenue officers is sought to be hit; but from 
this category of revenue officers village revenue officers known as lambardars, 
malguzars, patels, deshmukhs or by any other name, whose duty is to collect land 
revenue and who are remunerated by a share of, or commission on , the amount 
of land revenue collected by them , but who do not discharge any po ice func 
tons, are exempted . Therefore , the assistance rendered by those village revenue 
officers who are remunerated by a share or conmission on the amount of land 
revenue will not fall within the mischief of S . 123 ( 7 ) of the Act . Even in res 
pect of such village revenue officers, who are remunerated by a share of or 
commission on the amount of land revenue , it has been provided by cl. ( f ) that 
I they discharge any police functions, then they are not exempted from the 
category of revenue officers and any assistance rendered by a village revenue 
officer who is remunerated by a share of, or commission on , the amount of land 
revenue, and who also discharges police functions, if other conditions of s , 123 
( 7 ) are satisfied will amount to a corrupt practice . It is , therefore , clear that 
the vitiating factor so far as S . 123 ( 7 ) ( f ) is concerned is the discharging of any 
police functions, but for that discharge of police function village revenue officers . 
who are remunerated by a share of, or commission on , the amount of land 
revenue collected by them , will not come within the category of revenuc officers 
whose assistance is sought to be hit by S . 123 ( 7 ) . It in the case of a village 
revenue omcer , whose assistance would otherwise be unobjectionable , discharg 
ing of any police functions by such yillage revenue officer becomes objectionable , 
then it is clear that it is the discharging of police functions which is the vitiat 
Ing factor . It would rather lead to startling results if the discharge of police 
functions by & village revenue officer , who is remunerated by a share of , or 
commission on , the amount ot land revenue collected by him , would be objection 
able if he also happens to discharge police functions ; but if a Police Patel who 
does not discharge any other revenue functions but discharges the same kind 
of duties as the village revenue officer referred to in cl ( f ) . so far as police 
functions are concerned would not fall within cl. ( d ) . A person who has been 
appointed a Police Patel simplt citer cannot fall within cl, ( f ) of $ . 123 ( 7 ) : but 
at the same time so far as the police functions are concerned , both a police Patel 
simpli citer and a village revenue Patel, who is remunerated by a share of, or 
commission on , the amount of land revenue collected by him and who also dis 
charges police functions. would be hit by S . 123 ( 7 ) ( i ) . Under these circum 
stances, my conclusion that the wider meaning should be placed on the phrase : 
" members of the police forces " , gets some support from this interpretation of 
S . 123 ( 7 ) ( 1 ) of the Act. 


I may also point out that the word forces occurring in S . 123 ( 7 ) ( ai, means 
any organized group of persons, according to the dictionary meaning. In the 
Shorter Oxford Dictionary , the word " force " in this sense means " A body of 
armed men , an army. In pl. the troops of soldiers composing fighting strength 
of a kingdom or of a commander. A body of police , the force -policemen collec 
tively " . According to Welster s New Twentieth Century Dictionary , Second Edi 
tion , the word " force " , when used as a noun means " any organized group of 
soldiers, sailors etc .; any group of people organized for some activity ; as, a sales 
force . The dictionary meaning also throws some light on the correct interpreta 
tion to be placed on the words: "members of the police forces " occurring in S . 123 
( 7 ) ( d ) of the Act. 

In view of the mischief which is sought to be preyented by S . 123 , 7 and in 
view of the provisions of S . 123 ( 7 ) ( f ) and also in view of the dictionary Meaning 
of the word " force." , the words : " members of the police forces , in my opinion 
apply to all persons who are discharging police functions and who are organized 
in a body as such , In view of this interpretation of s . 123 ( 7 ) ( d ) . I will now 
examine the provisions of the Bombay Village Police Act, 1867, in order to see 
whether a Police Patel functioning under that Act falls within the category of 
"members of police forces " as thus interpreted By S . 3 of that Act it has been 
provided that the administration of the village - police throughout each district 
shall, under the control and direction of the State Government be exercised by

the Magistrate of the district , who may, with the sanction of the State Govern 
ment, delegate any portion thereof to any Magistrate with full powers having 
also revenue charge as an Assistant or Deputy Collector , Sub - section ( 1 ) of S . 5 
of that Act provides that the village -police in each village shall be under the 
charge of such person as the State Government shall appoint in writing to be 
Police Patel, and unless the State Government owing to the size of the village 
or other good cause , see fit to separate the appointments the State Government 
shall appoint the person discharging the duties of Revenue - patello be Police 
patel; and sub -section ( 2 ) of $ . 5 , provides for appointment of Police Patcls . 
Section 6 lays down that the Police Patel shall, subject to the orders of the 
Magistrate of the District , act under the orders of any other Executive Magistrate 
within whose local Jurisdiction his village is situated , whom he shall furnish 
with any returns or information called for, and keep constantly informed as to 
the state of crime and all matters concerned with the village - police , the health 
and general conditions of the community in his village. He shall also afford all 
Police Omcers every assistance in his power when called upon by thern for 
assistance in the performance of their duty. He shall further promptly obey and 
execute all orders and warrants issued to him by a Magistrate or Police Officer , 
shall collect and communicate to the Police Officer in charge of the District Police 
Station within the limits of which the village is situated , intelligence plecting 
the public peace , shall prevent within the limits of his village the commission 
offences and public nuisances, and shall detect and bring offenders therein to 
Justice. Under s . 8 , the Police Patel has to dispose of the village - establishment 
so as to afford the utmost possible security against robbery , breach of the peace 
and acts injurious to the public and to the village community, and shall report . 
to the Executive Magistrate to whose jurisdiction he is immediately suhordinate 
all Instances of misconduct or neglect committed by any member of the said 
establishment, Section 9 provides for penalties for neglect of duty by any Police 
Patel; and S . 10 provides that if a crime shall have been committed within the 
limits of the village, and the perpetrator of the crime has escaped or is nnt known 
the Police Patel shall forward immediate information to the Police Officer in 
charge of the District Police Station within the limits of which his village is 
situated , and shall himself proceed to investigate the matter , obtaining ull pro 
curable evidence relating to it which he shall forward to the said Officer . Under 
S . 11, the Police Patel has to hold an inquest in case of unnatural or sudden leath 
or a corpse found within his village, Section 12 provides that the police patel 
should apprehend the person , whom he believes , has committed serious offence 
and should forward such person to the Police Station within the limits of which 
his village is situated Section 13 provides that the Police Pate in making any 
inquiry coming within the scope of his duty shall have authority to call and 
examine witnesses, and record their statements, and to search for concealed arti 
cles , taking care that no search be made in a dwelling - house between sunset and 
sunrise without urgent occasion . He shall also have authority , in carrying out 
any search or any pursuit of supposed criminals , to enter and act within the 
limits of other villages, being bound however to give immediate information to 
the Police Patel thereof; and under $ . 19 , the Police Patel shall take charge of 
all unclaimed property found within his village, or made over to him under the 
provisions of the Bombay Police Act, 1951, and shall forthwith make a report to 
the Executive Magistrate to whom he is subordinate , and act thereafter as tre may 
be directed by the said Magistrate . unless the property be of a description 
coming within the provisions of the Cattle - trespass Act , 1871, or any law corres 
ponding to that Act in force , which case the Police Patel shall be guided by that 
enactment or law . 

This summary of the relevant provisions of the Bombay Village Police Act 
( VIII of 1867) , makes it clear that the Police Patel has the power to investigate 
to hold Invest to record evidence and statements and also the power to apprehend 
suspected oftenders . Under these circumstances , he discharges police functions 
when he functions as a Police Patel under the provisions of the Bombay Village 
Police Act , 1867. It is true that there is no section in this Act of 1867 coristitu 
ting a separate police force of village police Patels are members . It is also true 
that the provisions which are made in this Act for maintenance of the discipline 
amongst the village police are not as strict as are to be found , for example , under 
the Bombay Police Act , 1951, or under the Bombay special reserve pollce Act, 1951 
or under the Railway Protection Force Act, 1957 . Though the discipline may 
not be as strict as is to he found in the case of other police forces, there is a 
provision for the maintenance of the discipline of the village Po irr and merely 
on the ground that the discipline provided for under the Bombay Village Police 
Act, 1867, is not as strict as in the case of ordinary police or in the case of the 
State Reserye Police or in the Railway Protection Force , it does not mean that

- - - 


the police Patels are any the less members of a police force . They are discharg -- 
ing police functions and are permitted within certain limits to investigate and to 
carry on the Inquiry and collect evidence and carry on Investigation , 

Under these circumstances , I have come to the conclusion that within the 
wider meaning to be attached to the words "members of the police forces " , a 
Police Patel appointed and functioning under the Bombay Village l olice Act, 
1887, is a member of the Police forces . 

That takes me to the next question which is to be considered in connection 
with S . 123 ( 7 ) ( d ) of the Act , viz ., whether a Police Patel functioning as a Polling 
Agent at a Polling Station within his own jurisdiction can be said to be render 
ing assistance for the furtherance of the election prospects , of any candidate. 

In this connection Mr. Raru , on behalf of the petitioner , drew my attention to 
the decision of the Supreme Court in Dr, Y , S . Parinar y . Hira Singh , A . I. R . 
1959 S . C . 244. It must be pointed out that at the time when the Supreme Court 
delivered that judgment, the statutory presumption under Explanation ( 2 ) to 
S .123 ( 7 ) was still there, with the result that if any person worked as the Polling 
Agent of a candidate , he was to be deemed to render assistance to further the 
prospects of the election of that candidate . In Dr. Y . S . Parmar s ( supra ) the 
Supreme Court has laid down that all that S . 123 requires is that assistance shall 
be procured for furthering the election , Where the explanation applies, if a 
candidate has appointed a person to act as his polling agent and ho accordingly 
does so act , a statutory presumption arises that the candidate thereby procured 
that person s assistance in furtherance of the prospects of his election , and this 
irrespective of whether he intended to procure such assistance or not. Therefore, 
in the case of the appointment of a polling agent which comes within the explana 
tion the intention of the candidate in procuring the assistance is irrelevant. 
Similarly the knowledge of the candidate whether the person , whose service as 
his polling agent he has procured , is a member of the Armed forces or any of 
the other specified class of Government servants or not, is equally irrelevant and 
the Supreme Court has further held that no question of mens red or intention 
or knowledge of the candidate would arise in such cases . It inust be borne in 
mind that after the amendment of 1966 , the deeming provision of Explanation ( 2 ) 
to S . 123 ( 7 ) does not apply in case of polling agents or counting agents ; and 
because of this statutory amendment, Mr. Daru , on behalf of the petitioner, has 
relied on two other previous decisions of the Supreme Court . It is true that 
what happened in Dr. Y . S . Parmar s case was that the candidatc concerned 
signed a very large number of the forms prescribed by the rules framed under 
the Act for appointing polling agents , in blank and without setting out therein 
the name of any polling agent. as he had not then been able to make up his 
mind in view of the large number of polling stations as to who would be his 
polling agents at the various polling stations and made over these forms to one 
Kalyan Singh , who passed on three of them to Kashmira Singh , having inserted 
therein the words " polling station No. 13 , Sheopur" . Kashmira Singh Ajled in 
the name of Amar Singh as the polling agent in one of these forms on 25th May , 
1957, the day of polling : and Amar Singh , happened to be a member of the Armed 
Forces of the Union ; and in para 10 at page 247, A , K . Sarkar J . ( as he then 
was ) , delivering the judgment of the Supreme Court observed : -- 

" It is true that neither the appellant nor Kalyan Singh , nor even Kashmira 
Singh knew at the date of the appointment of Amar Singh that he was a member 
of the armed forces but the point now raised by the learned Advocate -General is , 
in our view , none the loss unsustainable . It over - looks the provisions of the second 
explanation . . . . " 

The contention which was repelled in para 10 was that procuring or obtaining 
by a candidate of any assistance for the furtherance of the prospects of his election 
from a person in the service of the Government as a member of the armed forces , 
would not amount to a corrupt practice unless that candidate knew that the 
person was in such Government service. This decision in Dr , Y . S . Parmar s case 
would apply to the facts of the present case in so far as the respondent allowed 
a rubber - stamp impression of his signature to be put down on various forms 
for the appointment of polling agents and he allowed these loring to be taken 
away by the workers of the Swatantra Party so that the names of the different 
polling agents may be filled and the forms may be submitted to the appropriate 
Presidint Officers of the Polling Stations concerned ; but the similarity of the 
present case and Dr. Y . S . Parmar s case does not go beyond that point. 

In Satya Dev V . Padam Dev ., A .I.R . 1954 S. C . 587 , the Supremc Court dealt 
with the provisions of S . 123 ( 8 ) of the Act, which were equivalent to S . 123 ( 7 ) 


2668 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1968 / JYAISTHA 18 . 1890 [PART II.. 

- - - - - 
as it stands today. In that case , the Supreme Court held that mere appointment 
of a Government servant within any on the categories mentioned in that section 
as a polling agent did not per se contravene the provisions of S . 123 (8 ) as it then 
stood , and then proceeded to observe : - 

" Nor is there anything in the nature of the duties of a polling agent, which 
necessarily brings him within the prohibition inacted in that section , The duty 
of a polling agent is merely to identify the voter, and that could not by itselt 
and without more , be said to further the election prospects of the candidate . So 
long as the polling agent confines himself to his work as such agent of merely 
identifying the voters , it cannot be said that S . 123 ( 8 ) has , in any manner , been 
infringed. 

It is argued for the appellant that leaving aside ihe world of theories and 
entering into the realm of practical politics, the appointment ct a Government 
servant as polling agent by one of the candidates must result in the dice being 
loaded heavily against the other candidate and that a situation might be con 
ceived in which the presence of a Government servant of rank and importance 
as polling agent of one of the candidates might prove to be a source of unfair 
election practices. But if that is established and if it is made out that the candi 
date or his agent had abused the right to appoint a Government servant as pol ing 
agent by explaciting the situation for furthering his eluciion prospects , then the 
matter can be dealt with as an infringement of S . 123 ( 8 ) ." 

This principle was affirmed by the Supreme Court when it dealt with the 
review of the above judgment; and the decision on the review application is 
reported in A . I. R . 1955 S . C . 6 . At page 8 , in para 10 of the report, Venkatarama 
Ayyar J., delivering the judgment of the Court, has observed : - - 

" Accordingly , we reaffirm the view taken by us that the appointment of a 
Government servant as polling agent does not, without more , contravene Section 
123 ( 8 ) . It is scarcely necessary to repeat our observation in the original judg 
ment that " If it is made out that the candidate or his agent had abused the right 
to appoint a Government servant as polling agent by exp oiting the situation for 
furthering his election prospects , then the matter can be dealt with as an in 
fringement of Section 123 ( 8 ) ," 

I may point out that in the instant case there is no evidence to show that the 
first respondent or his election agent had abused the right to appoint a Govern 
ment servant as a polling agent by exploiting the situation for furthering his 
election prospects . It is true that each of these three Polling Agents , viz .. Puna 
bhai Pratapsing of Chandpur, Kalubhai Haribhai of Parabia and Shanaji Punjaji 
Damor of Nava Village, acted as a pol ing agent within the limits of his own 
jurisdiction as a Police Patel. But merely because he go acted , it cannot be said 
that something more which is required by the Supreme Court to bring the casc 
within the mischief of S . 123 ( 7 ) , is also supplied . That something more must 
mean some action on the part of the polling agent or on the part of the candidato 
himself which would go to show that the Police Patel s in question had done 
something more than merely acting as Po ling Agent. Under these circumstances , 
the argument advanced on behalf of the petitioner on the basis of these two 
decisions of the Supreme Court cannot help him to advance his case further . 

The last question that now remains to be considered is whether as a matter 
of fact. the respondent obtained or procured any assistance of any one of the 
three Police Patels for furtherance of his election prospects . It is clear that so 
far as the word " procures " is concerned in S . 123 ( 7 ) of the Act, it indicates that 
there must be some positive action on the part of the candidate concerned before 
he can be said to procure assistance of any person ; but it is possible to argue that 
the word " obtains ” in S . 123 ( 7 ) means even the act of obtaining irrespective of 
any positive action on the part of the candidate or his election agent. In my 
opnion , the words ; " obtains" and " procures " occurring in S . 123 ( 7 ) mean one 
and the same thing viz ., any positive action on the part of the candidate concern 
ed in order to see that such assistance by any person in the prohibited categories 
was made available to him , 

A Division Bench of the Allahabad High Court in Moti Lal v. Mangla Prasad , 
AIR . 1958 Allahabad 794 , has taken the same view as I am taking . At page 797 
in para 11 of the report , it has been pointed out by the Division Bench as fol 
lows: 

"We think that the word obtain in S . 123 ( 7 ) has been used in the sense of 
the meaning which connotes purpose or effort behind the action of the candi 

date . The word has not been used in the sub - section in the sense of a mere 
: passive receipt of assistance without the candidate being even conscious of the 


- 


. 


- 


S 


. - 


- 


$ rc . 3( 11 )1 THE GAZETTE, OF INDIA : JUNE X . 1968 / 1VAISTHA 18 . 1990) 2669 

SH 
fact that the assistance has been rendered . In order to bring the case under sub 
section ( 7 ) , it must be shown that the candidate did sobie effort or performed 
some purposeful act in order to get the assistance . 

The receipt of assistance must be the result of either purpose or effort , which 
implies that the getting of the assistance should be the result of some conscious 
action of the candidate and not a mere passive happening of his getting the 
assistance . It further appears to be unreasonable to inpute to the Legislature 
an intention that the election of a candidate should be declared to be void , even 
though the candidate had no knowledge even of the fact that some person in the 
setvice of Government had taken into his head to assist a particular candidate ." 

With respect, I agree with these observations made by the learned Judges of 
the Allahabad High Court . It is , therefore, clear that ttiere must be some posl 
tive action or effort or purposes on the part of the respondent. before holding that 
the assistance rendered by the Police Patels in question was procured or obtain 
ed at the instance of the respondent. 


Out of the three Police Patels in connection with whom allegations have been 
made in the petition , so far as Shanaji Punjaji Damor of village Nava is concern 
ed , he himself has not been examined as a witness in this case , but petitioner s 
witness No . 19 , Prabhashanker Shivram Upadhyay, has stated in his evidence that 
at the time of the last general elections, he himself was a polling agent of the 
Congress Party at the Polling Booth at Masadra. Masadra is situaled in Sabar 
kantha District but it was included in Godhra Parliamentary Constituency acrord 
ing to this witness Prabhashanker , the Polling Agent on behalf of the Swatantra 
Party at Mosadra Polling Booth was Shanaji Punjaji Damor of village Nava , 
According to Prabhashanker Nava Village and Andharivadi village were included 
in the area for which the polling booth was at Masadra . He had also deposed 
that he had seen Shanaji Punjaji Damor carrying on propaganda of the Swatan 
tra Party at village Nava. That was the only occasion when he had seen Shanaji 
Punjaji Damor carrying on election propaganda of the Swatantra Party . He had 
heard Shanaji telling the electors at Nava to cast their votes for the Swatantra 
Party candidates and to bear the symbol of " Star " in mind when exercising their 
franchism Anart from this evidencn of Prabhashanker there is nothino on the 
record of this case which would go to show that the respondent had obtained or 
procured the assistance of Shanaji Punjaji Bamor with a purpose or deliberate 
design to obtain the services of that Government servant for furthering his own 
election prospects . Under these circumstances, so far as Shanaji Punjaji Damor 
is concerned , it cannot be said that any assistance contrary to the provisions of 
S . 123 ( 7 ) of the Act was obtaincd by the respondent for furtherance of his elec 
tion prospects . 


Ag regards Punabhai Pratapsing Patel and Kalubhai Haribhai Patel, the peli

Kalubhai Haribhai Petitioner s Witness No. 7, has aiso stated in his examina

the Swatantra Party candidates. According to Kalubhai Haribhai , the respon 
gent and Kanubhai were both present when Ramubal Bhatia talked to him on 
these lines and after talking like this all the three of then left the village. This 
is the only part of his evidence by which Kalubhai Haribhai wants to depose lo 
obtaining or procuring of assistance of this particular Police Patel by the respon 
dent. Exhihit No . 7 is the statement, dated 14th July , 1967, it has been signed by 
Kalubhai Haribhai on July 14 , 1967, before the Taluka Magistrate . He admit s 
that apart from the portions underlined in blue pencil on the original document, 
the rest of the statements set out in Ex. No . 7 are are correct In Ex . No. 7 he 
bas stated that before the date on which the first respondent appointed him as his 
polling agent and even afterwards he had carried on election work on behalf of 
Ramubhai Bhatia and the respondent in the villages of Parabia , Morwa -Hadap 
and surrounding villages at the instance of Ramubhai Bhatia and the respondent. 
Now , Kanubhai Talshi, whose evidence on this point cannot be relied upon , has 
noc stated in Ex . 6 anything about the respondent asking Kalubhai Haribhai to 
carry on propaganda or election work on behalf of the Swatantra Party candi 
dates . Moreover , the very fact that Kalubhai Haribhai swore an affidavit at the 
instance of the petitioner on or about July , 14 , 1987, after the present Elcction 
Petition was Aled , creates a doubt about the veracity of that witness . It appears 
from the record of this case that on 14th and 15th July , 1967, k number of per 
erns were taken before the Taluka Magistrate at the instance of the petitioner 
and all of them were identified before the Taluka Magistrato by one Jugubhai 
Solanki, a Congress Party worker . Thus, after the present petition was filed and 
the written statement had been put in , an attempt was made by the petitioner 
to obtain these affidavit and to fix their versions with a view to see that in the 
triai of the present election petition , the evidence of those witnesses would be 
according to the sworn affidavits and thus to fix , so to say , the evidence of these 
witnesses in a particular pattern . The swearing of those affidavits does create 
a suspicion as to whether those witnesses are telling the truth when they come 
before the Court and say that they had carried on the propaganda at the instance 
of the respondent. Even if they had carried on such propaganda in their own 
villages or roundabout areas, So far as Kalubhai Haribhai and Punabhat Pratap 
sing are concerned , the cross - examination with reference to the contents of Exs . 7 
al. ci 8 respectively goes to show that neither of them is a reliable witness . Exp. 7 
and 8 are word to word the same and each of the winesses has stated that it 
was through omission on the part of the scribe or the petitionei -writer at the 
Taluka Magistrate s office, who wrote out these andavits. that it was not nen 
tioned that 9 or 10 days before the date of the election Rambubhai Bhatia and 
the respondent had come to the house of Kalubhai Haribhai and Punabhai Protan 
sine . In my opinion , it would be extremely hazardous to rely on thr testimony 
of Kalubhal or Punabhai for the purpose of holding that the respondent chtain 
ed or procured the assistance of either of these two Police Patcls for the further 
anon of prospects of his clection . The petitioner has not been able to lead any 
rellable evidence on this point and the evidence that has been in fact lcd or the 
point is not free from suspicion and is not trustworthy . 


Under these circumstances . I hold that the petitioner has failed to establish 
that the respondent obtained or procured the assistance of talubhai Fioribhai or : 
Shanaji Punjaji Damor for the furtherance of his election prospects . The fact 
that these threr persons were Police Patels is not disputed , the 
fact that each of them acted as a polling agent of the respondent 
is again not disputed : but what is disputed is that the respondent obtain 
ed or procured the assistance of these three individuals in the sense of any 


Sec . 3( ii)] 


THE GAZEITE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1968/ JYAISTIIA 18. 1890 


2671 


positive action on the part of the respondent, which would lead to such assistance 
being received from any one of these three Police Patels . Reliable evidence on 
that crucial aspect of the matter is lacking and , therefore, the petitioner s case 
on this aspect of the matter must fail and it must be held that it cannot be said 
that the respondent procured or obained the assistance of any of the threc Police 
Patels , viz ., Kalubhai Haribhai of Parabia , Punabhai Pratapsing of Chandpur and 
Shanaji Punjaji Danor of Village Nava , for the furtherance of his own clectic . 
prospects . This issue must , therefore , be decided agaiſist the petitioner , 


I, therefore, answer the issues as follows: 
Issue No. 

Finding. 

Not pressed . 
2 . In the negative as to first part ; second part does not arise . 
3 . Not pressed 
4 . Not preaged . 
3 . In the negative as to First part second part does not arise , 
8 . In the negative as to all its parts . 
7. In the negalive as to Arst part (in view of my judgment in Elec., Pet. 

No 24 , 1967 , decided on October 23 , 1967 ) , 
8 . Not pressed . 

9 . In the negative. 
The result, therefore, is that this petition falls and is dismisser). The peti 
tii:ner to pay the custs of the respondent. Advocate s fees Axed at Rs. 1, 400 
( Rupees One Thousand Nine Hundred ) .

S . O . 1962 . - -In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act , 1947 
(14 of 1947 ) . the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of 
the Industrial Trlbunal No. 2 , Dhanbad , in the industrial dispute between the 
employers in relation to the Digwadih Collery of Mesor8 . Tata Iron ind Steel 
Company Limited , Jamadoba, Post Office Jealgora , District Dhanbad and their 
workmen. which was received by the Central Government on the 21st May , 1988 .

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL (NO . 2 ) 

AT DHANBAD 
In the matter of a reference under Section 10 ( 1 ) (d ) of the Industrial 
Disputes Act , 1947. 

Reference No . 90 of 1967 . 
PARTIES : 
Employers in relation to the Digwadih Colliery of Messrs Tata Iron and 

Steel Company Limited , Jamadoba , Post Office Jealgora , District 
Dhanbad . 

AND 
Their Workmeni . 
PRESENT : 

Shri Nandagiri Venkata Rao . - Presiding Officer. 
APPEARANCES : 

For the employers. — Shri S . S . Mukherjee , Advocate . 
For the Workmen . - Shri Pritish Chanda , President, Tata Collleries Workers 

Union . 
State : Bihar 

Industry : Coal. 
Dhanbad , dated 16th May , 1988 . 

AWARD 
The Central Government, being of opinion that an industrial dispute exists 
between the employers in relation to the Digwadih Colliery of Messrs Tata Iron 
and Steel Company Limited , Jamadoba , Post Office Jealgora , District Dharbad 
and their workmen by its order No . 2 / 122 / 65-LRII dated 1st Decenzber , 1965 
referred to the Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad under Section 
10 ( 1 ) ( d ) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 for adjudication the dispute in 
respect of the matter specified in the schedule annexed thereto . The schedule is 
extracted below : 

SCHEDULE 
“ Whether the management of the Digwadih Colliery of Messrs Tata Iron 

and Steel Company Limited was justified in dismissing Shri Krishna 
Bandhu , Trolleyman ( T . No. 33675 ) from its service with eilect from 

the 16th June, 1965 ? If not, to what relief is the workman entitled ? " 
2 . The Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad registered the 
reference as reference No . 197 of 1965 on its Ale . Workmen Aled their statement 
of demands. While it was pending before the Central Government Industrial 
Tribunal, Dhanbad the proceeding was transferred to this Tribunal by the Central 
Government by its order No. 8 / 25 / 67- LRII dated 8th May 1967 under Section 
33B ( 1 ) of the Industrial Disputes Act , 1947. Consequently , the reference is 
renumbered on the Ale of this Tribunal as reference No. 90 of 1967. Employers 
Aled their statement of demands as well as rejoinder . 

3 . It is not in dispute that Shri Krishna Bandhu , Trolleyman ( T . No. 33675 ) 
(hereinafter referred to as the affected workman for the sake of conyenience ) 
was an employee of the employers colllery . It is also admitted that he was 
served with a charge - sheet alleging misconduct viz . riotous and indecent behaviour 
in terms of Clause 19 ( 5 ) of the Certified Standing Orders, a domestic enquiry 
was made against him in which he was found guilty and in consequence thereof 
he was dismissed from service. The case of the workmen is that the dismissal 
of the affected workman was an act of victimisation , that when the affected 
workman demanded his due bus, steamer and railway fare, the Senior Welfare 
Omcer unnecessarily came into temper , flared up and abused the affected workman 
in bad language and turned him out of office , that on the report of the affected 
workman the management did not take any action and instead brought a false 
charge against him , that no guilty was proved against the affected workman in 
the show of enquiry made against him , that the Anding of the Enquiry Officer 
was pervera and that the dismissal of the affected workman was uncalled for 
and unjust. The employers took the stand that they had conducted proper 
were represented by Shri Pritish Chanda , President, Tata Collieries Works 
enquiry and dismissed the affected workman for the misconduct. The workinen 


2674 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1968 / JYAIŞTHA 18, 1890 


[PART II 


Union and the employers by Shri S . S . Mukherjee, Advocate . On 14th February , 
1968 , as no one was present on behalf of the workmen , the case proceeded against 
them in accordance with Rule 22 of the Industrial Disputes ( Central ) Rules, 1957, 
the employers examined a witness and marked Ext. Mi to M15 and arguments 
were heard and the award was reserved . On 16th February , 1968 the workmen 
submited an application to reopen the case and give them an opportunity to lead 
their evidenc ? Arguments were heard in respect of the application and the 
application was allowed on 11th March , 1988 although the employers challenged 
the jurisdiction of the Tribunal to consider the application relying upon the 
decision of the Calcutta High Court in Gungaram Tea Company, Ltd ., V8. Second 
Labour Court and another ( 1967- II -LLJ 325 ) . On behalf of the workmen a 
witness was examined and Ext. W1 wag marked . 

4 . Exts . Mi to M15 are proved by MW1, the Assistant Chief Personnel and 
Welfare Officer . These exts . are concerned with the charge -sheet and domestic 
enquiry held against the affected workman . These documents are not seriously 
denied by the workmen . There is no rebuttal evidence to contradict them . It is 
an admitted case that the domestic enquiry was held against the affected workman . 
Ext . M1, the charge- sheet clearly states that the affected workman had committed 
misconduct viz . riotous and indecent behaviour in terms of Clause 19 (5 ) of the 
Certified Standing Orders . The facts in it were that on 4th May, 1966 at 8 . 30 am . 
the affected workman entered the office of the Senior Welfare Omcer and started 
shouting as to why the affected workman was being paid less return railway 
fare and that inspite of the Senior Welfare Officer explaining to him that his 
claim was not correct , the affected workman flared up and started shouting and 
threatening him with dire consequences. In the reply submitted to the charge 
sheet the affected workman did not deny that he had been to the office of the 
Senior Welfare Officer , but put a different version of the occurrence. According 
to him it was the Senior Welfare Officer himself that used bad language against 
the affected workman for demanding proper and due return fare . The ailected 
workman is examined as WW1. He did not deny the domestic enquiry held 
against him or that he was given a fair chance to cross examine the management s 
witnesses and lead his defence . During the enquiry the affected workman sub 
mitted an application to the Enquiry Omcery to read and explain to him the 
statements recorded in English . : The Enquiry Officer allowed the request and 
directed Shri B . N . Ghatak to explain the depositions to the aitected workman . 
There is endorsement by Shri B . N . Ghatak on the depositions that they were 
explained by him to the affected workman in Bengalt. The witness were cross 
examined also by the affected workman . The statement of the affected workman 
was also recorded . The affected workman examined Ave witnesses in his defence. 
Ext. M10 is the report of the enquiry which shows that the Enquiry Officer had 
discussed the evidence at length and ultimately found the affected workman 
guilty of the charge levelled against him . On this the Manager submitted the 
note, Ext. M11 recommending dismissal of the affected workman . Ext. M12 is the 
order of the ACME & Agent dismissing the affected workman with effect from 
16th June, 1965. The order shows that the ACME & Agent had gone through 
the charge - sheet and its connected papers and satisfied himself that the charge 
levelled against the affected workman had been satisfactorily proved . Ext. M13 
is the dismissal letter dated 10th June, 1965 under the signature of the ACME 
stating that the affected workman was dismissed from the service of the employers 
with effect from 16th June , 1965. This evidence is neither denied nor rebutted . 
On this material I do not find any flaw in the domestic enquiry . I And that the 
affected workman was glven a falr chance to participate in the enquiry and 
defending himself. From the report of enquiry , Ext. M10 and the evidence 
recorded during the domestic enquiry I find no substance in the contention that 
the findings arrived at by the Enquiry Officer were perverse . Shri Pritish Chanda , 
the learned representative of the workmen has argued that the demand of the 
return railway , bus and steamer fare by the affected workman was justified . But 
that point had no bearing on the enquiry before the Enquiry Officer . The only 
matter before him wag whether the affected workman was guilty of riotous and 
indecent behaviour as was alleged in the charge - sheet, Ext, M1. It is then con 
tended that immediately after the occurrence the affected workman had submitted 
an application to the Manager complaining against the Senior Welfare Officer and 
that the Manager did not hold any enquiry in the matter . But Ext. M16 is a 
reply sent to the affected workman by the Chief Mining Engineer stating that to 
enquire into the allegations made by him in his applications dated 4th May , 1965 
and 31st May, 1965 , Chief Personnel & Welfare Officer had visited the colliery on 
17th May , 1965 and despite prior information given to him , the affected workman 
did not turn up and that the enquiry was conducted in his absence and the con 
clusion was arrived at that in order to avoid the consequences of his misbehaviour 


SEC. 3( il)] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1968 / JYAISTHA 18, 1890 


267 


with the Senior Welfare Officer , the affected workman had taken recourse to the 
complaint. The affected workman has not denied the letter nor did he lend any 
rebuttal evidence . The burden of the statement filed on behalf of the workmen 
is that the dismissal of the affected workman was nothing but victimisation owing 
to the rivalry between the Tata Collieries Workers Union and the Colliery Mazdoor 
Sangh , the affected workman being an office bearer and Assistant Secretary of 
the former. But in his deposition as AW1, the affected workman has comeforth 
with a new case altogether . He deposed that on 10th April, 1965 while he was 
collecting subscription for his union the Senior Welfare Officer himself and his 
chaprasis prevented him from doing so and called in police, etc . But no whisper 
of this case was made at any time before , either before this Tribunal or the 
Enquiry Oficer . Obviously , this case cannot be relied upon to any extent. On 
this material I And ample justification for the employers in dismissing the affected 
workman . 

5 . I, therefore, hold that the management of the Digwadih Collery of M / s . Tata 
Iron & Steel Company Limited was justified in dismissing the affected workman , 
Shri Krishna Bandhu , Trolleyman ( T . No. 33675 ) from its service with effect from 
the 16th June , 1965 and , consequently , he is not entitled to any relief. The award 
Is made accordingly and submitted under Section 15 of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 1947. 

$ d . / - N . VENKATA RAO, 

Presiding Officer , 
Central Government Industrial Tribunal (No. 2 ) at Dhanbad .

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL - CUM 

LABOUR COURT (No. 1 ), DHANBAD . 
In the matter of an application under Section 33 ( 2) (b ) of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 1947 

Application No. 60 of 1960 

AND 

Reference No. 11 of 1968 
PARTIES : 
Employers in relation to Kamptee Colllery of M / s . Oriental Coal Company 
Limited , P . O . Kamptee, Distt. Nagpur - -- Applicant.

Shri Kameshwar Viswakarma, S / o . Dharamdeo Viswakarma, Temp. Addre...

For the Applicant. — None.

Dhanbad , dated , the 10th April 1968 

ORDER 


The employers in relation to Kamptee Colllery of M / s . Oriental Coal Co. Ltd ., 
P . O . Kamptee , Dist. Nagpur (Maharashtra ) Aled an application under Seculon 33 ( 2 ) , 
( b ) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (herein after referred to as the Act) for 
approval of dismissal of Shri Kameshwar Viswakarma on account of the pendency 
of reference No . 23 of 1963 . That application was rejected on the ground that Shri 
Viswakarma had not been made a party . The employers have since filled the pre 
sent application under the same provision for approval of the dismissal. It has 
heen stated in paragraph 9 of the application that it has been Aled because a 
reference bearing No . 23 of 1963 between the applicant and its workmen of the 
colliery is pending before this Tribunal. 

The application was placed before me at Nagpur on the 27th December , 1967) 
It was Shri Bobde who was then appearing on behalf of the employers who brought 


2678 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1968 / JYAISTHA 18 , 1890 


[ Part II-- 


to my notice the fact that, while this application was pending before me, the 
Central Government had , by Its Order No. 5 / 20 / 66 -LRII, dated the 8th November , 
1966 , made a reference to the Central Government Industrial Tribunal at Bombay 
in which the question raised was whether the dismissal of Kameshwar Vishwa 
karma was justified . In view of this fact, Shri Bobde and Shri Khambhare who 
Appeared on behalf of the workman requested me to adjourn the hearing of the 
application so that both matters might be placed before one and the same Tribunal 
for simultaneous disposal. 


The Government of India has since then transferred the reference vide Order 
No. 5 / 20 / 66 -LRII dated the 3rd February, 1968 which was numbered as reference 
No. 53 of 1966 in the Central Government Industrial Tribunal at Bombay to this 
Tribunal. The Government has further directed that the proceeding will continue 
from the stage at which it has been transferred . The reference has been numbr 
ed here as reference No. 11 of 1968. 


It seems that the parties have entered into a compromise and the entire dispute 
between them has been settled . The employers are the applicant. Shri Kaneshwar 
Vishwakarma is opposite party No. 1 , Maharashtra Pradesh Rashtriya Khadan 
Kamgar Sangh Is opposite party . No. 2 and Kamptee Koyala Khadan Kamgar 
Sangh is opposite Party 3 . Opposite party No. 1 has fleld an application pray 
Ing that in view of the settlement between the parties he does not wish to contest 
the employers application and his dismissal may be approved . Opposite party 
No . 3 has filed a separate application in which prayer has been made that the ap 
plication of the employers for approval of the Tribunal to the dismissal of opposita 
party No. I may be granted and dismissal approved . Opposite Party 
NO . 3 has further Aled an application in which prayer has been made that, 
In view of the settlement between the parties , the Tribunal may give an award to 
the reference in line and in terms of the settlement with these three applications, 
the agreement between the parties and the terms of settlement arrived at bet 
ween them have also been Aled . The substance of the compromise in that the 
management has paid in presence of the Asstt . Labour Commissioner , Nagpur a sum 
of Rs. 1, 800 / - as an ex - gratia payment to the workman , Shri Kameshwar Vishwa 
karma and he, on the other hand , has agreed that his dismissal should stand . 

I have no doubt that the compromise is genuine . It is also fair , The Asstt . 
Labour Commissioner, Nagpur has made an endorsement at the foot of the petition 
that money was paid before him . 

wb 
In the circumstances mentioned above , I dispose of the reference according to 
the compromise, which will form part of the award . Let it be submitted to the 
Govt . of India . 

The application is also disposed of in this way that the dismissal of Kameshwar 
Vishwakarma by the management of the Colliery is approved , in accordance wit .. 
the compromise between the parties . 

(Sa.) KAMLA SAMAI, 

Presiding Oncer, 
Central Govt. Industrial Trlbunal-Cum - Labour

Shri Kameshwar Viswakarma General Mazdoor in Electrical Department was

before the Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad for approval of the

action . The same application is pending before the Trlbunal under Reference No.
