[TAKT II 


New Delhi, the 28th May 1968 
S . O . 1964 , ...In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act , 1947 
( 14 of 1947) , the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of 
the Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad , in the industrial dispute between the employers 
in relation to the East Ghanoodih Colliery of Messrs East Ghanoodth Colliery 
Company ( Private ) Limited , Post Offce Jharla , District Dhanbad and their work 
men , which was received by the Central Government on the 22nd May , 1968 . 


BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL -CUM 

LABOUR COURT (NO . 1) , DHANBAD . 
In the matter of a reference Under Section 10 ( 1 ) (d ) of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947. 

Reference No. 55 of 1967. 
PARTIES : 
Employers in relation to the East Ghanqodih Colllery of _ Messrs Er 

Ghanoodih Colllery Company (Private ) Limited , P . O . Jharia , 
District Dhanbad . 

And 
Thelr Workmen . 
PREBENT : 

Shri Kamla Sahal.- Presiding Omcer. 
APPEARANCES : 
For the Employers. — Shri B . P . Dabral, Chief Personnel Oficer of the 

Company . 
For the Workmen . - Shri B . K . Lath , Advocate. 
STATE : Blhar. 

INDUSTRY : Coal. 
Dhanbad , dated the 15th May , 1968 . 

AWARD 
The Government of India has, by its Order No. 2 / 124 / 67-LRII, dated 18th 
November, 1967, referred this industrial dispute for adjudication to this Tribunal. 
The schedule attached to the reference is as follows:- - 

SCHEDULE 
: " Whether the management of the East Ghanoodſh Collery of Messrs East 

Ghanoodih Colliery Company (Private ) Limited , Post Office Jharla 
(Dhanbad ) was justified in not allowing employment to Sarvashri 
Adhin Singh and Gangadhar Singh , Mine Loaders with effect from 
the 14th June, 1967 ? If not, to what relief are the workmen entitled ? " 


2 . Both the parties have agreed that the name of the Colliery does not begin 
with " EAST" but it is simply Ghanoodih Colliery , East Section . The description 
given by the parties in agreement with each other may be assumed to be correct. 


3 . The workmen , Shri Adhin Singh and Shri Gangadhar Singh represented by 
the General Secretary , Krantikari Koyala Mazdoor Sangh , Aled a written statement 
on 30th December , 1967. They stated therein that the workmen were pennanent 
employers of the Collery and had put in a number of years of service, that the 
workmen were arbitrarily stopped from their duties on 14th June, 1967 without 
any Charge- Sheet and without assigning any reason and that the workmen have 
been victimized for their Union activities . 


4 . The employers Aled a written statement on 17th January, 1968 when they 
had not yet received the written statement Aled on behalf of the Union . In that 
written statement, they stated that the Reference was misconceived and bad in 
law , that the workmen concerned had no locus standi to raise the dispute in - as 
much as they were not workmen under the Industrial Disputes Act on the date 
of the dispute , that Gangadhar Singh was appointed on 30th January, 1967 as a 
Machine Loader on a permanent substantive post as a probationer but he absented 
himself with effect from 13th June, 1967 before the expiry of his probationary 
period and hence he was treated as having abandoned his service and that Adhin 


SEC. $ (11)] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1968 / JYAISTHA 18 , 1890 


2681 


Singh was appointed on 3rd February , 1967 as a Machine Loader on a permanent 
substantive post as a probationer but he started absenting himself with effect 
froin 30th May , 1967 and hence he was also treated as having abandoned his 
service . 

5 . In another written statement Alled by the employers on 30th January, 1968 , 
they stated that they have never been aware of the Union activities of the workmen 
and that there is , therefore , no question of their being victimized for such activities. 

6 . At the hearing , the parties have Aled some documents and they have also 
examined some witnesses. 


7 . The employers having admitted that Gangadhar Singh was appointed on 
permanent substantive post with effect from 30th January , 1967 and Adhin Singh 
was appointed on permanent substantive post with effect from 3rd February , 1967, 
the first question which arises for consideration is whether the appointment of 
the two workmen was on probation for a particular period . The letters of 
appointment of the workmen have not been produced nor have the office copies 
of those letters been produced . Shri Vikramaditya Jha ( M . W . 2 ) has produced 
a Register of employees (Ext. M2). The name of Gangadhar Singh appears in 
this Register . The witness has stated that Ext. M - 2 ( a ) is the thumb impression 
of Gangadhar Singh , but that fact is disputed . Besides, the Register does not 
bear the signature of the Manager or the Assistant Manager on any page. Shri 
B . K . Lath , who has appeared on behalf of the workmen , has pointed out that, 
under Clause 41 ( 6 ) of the Coal Mines Regulations, 1957, the Manager has to 
examine all registers among other records which are to be kept under the Act 
and has to countersign the signature on the Register though he may delegate this 
duty by an order in writing to an undermanager . Apart from the defect of 
absence of the signature of the manager or undermanager , the alleged thumb 
impression [ Ext. M - 2 ( a ) ] is so faint and undercipherable that it cannot be accepted 
as a genuine thumb- impression of Gangadhar Singh . In any case , there is 
nothing in this Register to show that Gangadhar Singh was appointed on prcbu 
tlon . No other document has been Aled to prove that either Gangadhar Singh 
or Adhin Singh was appointed on probation . M . W . 2 has said nothing on this 
point. Shri $ . V . Krishnamurty , ( M . W . 1 ) has been working as the Manager of 
Ghanoodih Colliery , East Section , from June, 1963 . He says that appointment 
is made in his Colliery is the first instance on a temporary, badll or probation 
basis , but he has not ascerted that either Gangadhar Singh or Adhin Singh was 
appointed on probation . I am , therefore , unable to hold that either of those 
workmen was on probation on or about the date on which each of them is alleged 
to have abandoned or been stopped from service . 

8 . Shri Gangadhar Singh has examined himself as W . W . 1 . He has stated 
that he has been working in the colliery from 1964 but previously he was on a 
temporary basis . It may be that, as shown by the register Ext. M - 2 , he was put 
on a permanent basis from 30th January, 1967. In that case also , he could not 
be put on probation at that stage. 

9 . Admittedly , no charge was framed and no enquiry was held against the 
workmen in question . The question for consideration which , therefore , arises at 
this stage is whether a workman can cease to be in service simply because he 
absents himself from his duties without any further action being taken by the 
employers. In other words, can a workman be simply ignored or can he bę 
deemed to have been thrown out of service unceremoniously because he absents 
himself for a day or two ? Shri Krishnamurty - - M . W . 1 - says that when a 
workman starts absenting himself regularly , the Register -Keeper makes a note 
that he is no longer in service . This is a curious procedure . A workman cannot 
be deemed to have abandoned service simply because of his absence for a day 
or two. Realising this situation , M . W . 1 added that the Register -Keeper makes 
enquiry from the Manager whether the workman is or is not in service and then 
the Manager gives him the correct position , 

10 . The Manager ( M . W . 1 ) says that the Arst news which he got about the 
grievancc of Gangadhar Singh was from the communication ( Ext. M - 3 ) Irom the 
AL. C . ( C ). This is dated 29th August , 1987 . Had the services of the workmen 
in question becn terminated with effect from 14th June, 1967 because of their 
voluntary absence , surely he must have learnt about it before 29th August, 1967. 
I, therefore, hold that the workmen did not voluntary absent themselves from 
service . 

11 . This may be considered from another aspect Clause 13 of the Company s 
Standing Orders ( Ext. M - 1 ) provides that if an employee remains absent without 


2682 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1968 / JYAISTHA 18 , 1890 


[PART II 


properly approved leave he will render himself liable for disciplinary action . If 
the two workmen in question had made themselves llable for such disciplinary 
action , charge should have been framed against them , enquiry should have been 
made and formal orders of termination of service should have been passed against 
them . The Manager - M . W . 1 - has stated that he did not issue a notice to 
Gangadhar Singh or Adhin Singh terminating their service on the ground of 
abandonment of services by them and that , so far as he remembers , no charge 
sheet was issued to them . 

12. In the circumstances mentioned above, I accept the workmen s case that 
the management stopped them from their work from 14th June, 1967. 

13 . Shri Dabral, who has appeared on behalf of the employers, has argued 
that the counterfoil receipt - Ext . W . 1418 not genuine and that the workmen 
liave not proved that they are members of the Union known as Krantikari Koyala 
Mazdoor Sangh , much less that the Management of the Company knew of their 
activities as members of that Union . It is unnecessary to consider this argument 
because, as I have already held , the management stopped them from working and 
this the management was not entitled to do. 

14 . In view of what I have said above, I am clearly of the opinion that the 
management of the Colliery Company in question was not justified in not allowing 
employment to Sarvashri Adhin Singh and Gangadhar Singh , Machine Loaders, 
with effect from 14th June, 1967. They are , therefore , entitled to be re -instated 
as Machine Loaders with effect from 14th June, 1967 with their back wages and 
continuity of service. 

15 . This is my award . Let it be submitted to the Central Government under 
Section 15 of the Industrial Disputes Act , 1947. 

Sd . / - KAMLA SAĦAL, 

Presiding Officer, 
Central Govt. Industrial Tribunal-cum -Labour 

Court, No. 1 , Dhanbad .

Employers in relaion to Messrs. Hindustan 

Sabarigiri Project . 


Construction 


Company Ltd ., 


AND 


Their Workmen .

For the Employers . — Shri K . V . R . Shenol of Messrs . Menon and Pa . 

Advocates and Shri B . Narayanaswamy, Advocate .

STATE : Kerala 

INDUSTRY : Stone Quarrying. 
Dated 25th April , 1968 

AWARD 


The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour and Employment have by 
their order No . 36 / 33 / 65 -LRI dated 20th November , 1966 referred to this tribunal 
for adjudication the industrial dispute existing between the employers in relation 
to the stone quarry of Meysrs . Hindustan Construction Company, Pattanamthitta , 
District Quilon and their workmen in respect of the matters specified in the 
following schedule : 

SCHEDULE 
" ( 1 ) Whether the workmen employed in the stone quarry at Pattanamthit , 

District Quilon of Messrs . Hindustan Construction Company Limited 

are entitled for bonus for the years 1962 - 1963 and 1963 - 1964 ? 
(2 ) If so, to what extent ?" 
2 . The employers — the Hindustan Construction Company Ltd . , were engaged 
in the work of construction of a dam and a tunnel at Sabarigiri. In connection 
with the dam work there was an adjacent stone quarry in which the employer 
company had engaged about 388 workmen . The construction of the dam started 
In the year 1962 and was continuing . It was expected to be completed in the 
year 1966 . The workmen employed by the company including the stone quarry 
workers had through the Secretary of the Sabarigiri Project Construction Em . 
ployees Union sent a charter of demands about bonus, onam advance , dearness 
allowance , issue of service cards etc ., and the matter was also represented to the 
Corciliation Offlcer. Negotiations were held and there was a settlement regarding 
almost all the demands including the bonus dispute. By the settlement the 
management agreed to pay bonus to all the workmen including the employees 
engaged in the stone quarry equivalent to ten days wages per year in full and 
Anal settlement of the claim . But subsequently the union again raised the same 
question about bonus for the years 1962 -63 and 1963 -64 in relation to the quarry 
workers and it has been referred to this tribunal. 

3 . The employers have by their written statement contended that in respect 
of the present dispute conciliation proceedings were held before the Deputy Labour 
Officer and Conciliation Officer , Pattanamthitta who was camping at Kakki in the 
presence of the representatives of the employers and the workmen and pursuant 
to the discussion that took place at that conciliation meeting a settlement was 


vu 


Src , 8( ii)] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1968 /JYAISTUA 18 , 1890 


2685 


arrived at. As per the settlement with reference to the demand for honus for 
the years 1962 -63 and 1963 -64 it was agreed that all the workmen should be paid 
honus equivalent to 10 days wages ( consolidated ) per year in respect of the said 
two years in full and final settlement of the claim of the workers for bonus. 
Pursuant to the said settlement the company even paid the agreed bonus to all 
its workmen both in the dam , tunnel work and stone quarry and all the workers 
without any exception have received the payment of this agreed bonus. They 
have contended that thus the demand for bonus in respect of the years 1962 -63 
And 1963 - 64 have been fully and finally settled as per the settlement dated 16th 
June, 1965. The workers were also paid the agreed amounts and they are not 
entitled to any aditional amount of bonus for the said two years. 


4 . After the receipt of the reference notices were issued to the General 
Secretary , Sabarigiri Project Construction Employees Union P . O . R . Perunad , 
District Kottayam , Kerala dated 4th December, 1985 , 20th December , 1965 , 29th 
January , 1966 , 26th February, 1966 , 3rd March , 1986, 23rd March , 1967 and 14th 
February , 1968. These notices were received by the General Secretary of the 
Sabarigiri Project Construction Employees Union but he has neither put in any 
appearance nor Aled any statement of claim , Once by his application dated 15th 
June, 1966 the General Secretary of the Union had requested for extension of 
time to Alle the statement of claim on behalf of the workers and though time was 
granted no written statement of claim has been Aled so far. The office had also 
forwarded to the Secretary of the Union a copy of the written statement of the 
company along with the letter dated 29th March , 1966 . The same has been 
received by the Secretary of the union but he has not taken any steps to Alę any 
statement of claim . 


5 . Notice of the hearing of this dispute in this sitting of the Tribunal at 
Ernakulam has been duly received by the General Secretary of the Union but he 
has remained absent. It is clear from the record of the conciliation proceedings 
that the workmen were represented by the Sabarigiri Project Construction 
Employees Union P . O . R . Perunad , District Qullon , Kerala before the Conciliation 
Officer The order of reference shows that the General Secretary , Sabarigiri 
Project Construction Employees Union , P . O . R . Perunad , District Kottayam . 
Kerala represented the workmen concerned in this reference about the demand 
for bonus for the two years in question and the notices were issued to the General 
Secretary of the Sabarigiri Project Construction Employees Union to the address 
mentioned in the order of reference . It is also clear from the record that the 
notices issued from the office of the Tribunal have been received by the union and 
althouogh the notice of hearing on 4th March , 1968 has been duly received by the 
General Secretary of the union he has remained absent. In spite of the notice dated 
8th April, 1968 and the hearing dated 22nd April, 1968 the union did not appear nor 
did it put forth any written statement and considering the conduct of the Union in 
not appearing and taking part it appears that they are not interested in the proceed 
ings of the reference and hence the matter is heard e.c -parte . 


6 . The employers have by their written statement contended that the dispute 
regarding the demand for bonus for the years 1962 -63 and 1963 -64 between 
themselves and the workmen including the stone quarry labourers has been 
settled during the conciliation proceedings. It was agreed by them to pay to all 
the workmen bonus equivalent to 10 days wages per year in respect of the said 
two years in full and final settlement of the claim . Along with the written 
statement they have produced a copy of the memorandum of settlement dated 
16th June, 1965 which purports to have been arrived at during the conciliation 
proceedings before the Conciliation Officer, Pattanamthitta camp Kakki, In the 
terms of settlement the very first item is regarding bonus which states :- - 


" 1 . Bonus, — It is agreed that all workmen shall be paid bonus equivalent 

to 10 days wages per year in full and Anal settlement of the claim 
of the workers for bonus." 


The employers in their written statement have stated that according to these terms 
of settlement they have made payments of the agreed amounts of bonus to the 
workers including the stone quarry workers . The union has not Aled any written 
statement and there is no reason to disbelieve the company s contention in the 
absence of any denial on the part of the workmen . It is clear from the copy of 
the settlement that the dispute has been settled and it appears that because of 
this settlement the workers are not interested and do not want to take part in 
the present proceedings . 


2686 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8, 1968 / JYAJSTHA 18 , 1890 


[l art II -- 


7 . As per the provisions of Rule 20 of the Industrial Disputes (Central Rules , 
1957, in cases where the workmen who are members of any trade union or associa 
tion are parties to the dispute the service of notice on the Secretary or on the 
principal officer of the union or association shall be deemed to be service on such 
persons. The Secretary has not remained present and has not shown any cause 
for his failure to file any written statement or attend the proceedings. Rule 22 
of the Industrial Disputes ( Central) Rules, 1957, enables the authority to hear 
proceedings ex parte . It says : - - 
" If without sufficient cause being shown , any party to proceedings before 

a Board , Court, Labour Court Tribunal, National Tribunal or 
Arbitrator fails to attend or to be represented the Board , Court, Labour 
Court, Tribunal, National Tribunal or Arbitrator may proceed as ir 

the party had duly attended or had been represented ." 
8 . The learned Counsel for the employers has contended that the reference 
pertains to the demand of bonus for the years 1962 -63 and 1963-64 . Their Lord 
ships of the Supreme Court have struck down the provisions of section 33 of the 
Payment of Bonus Act, 1965 which had enabled the workers to obtain bonus for 
the two years before the introduction of the act under the Act and as the workmen 
know full well that they will not be entitled to claim any bonus for the two years 
and hence they have remained absent. Shri Narayanaswamy the learned 
Advocate on behalf of the management has further argued that as there was a 
settlement arrived at between the parties before this reference the management 
has already paid the bonus to the workmen involved in this dispute . They have 
taken advantage of the settlement and they are not now entitled to claim anything 
further. It has been further argued that in spite of the several notices issued to 
the union they have not put forth any statement of claim which clearly showed 
that the workmen were satisfied with the bonus recelved by then under the 
agreement and they are not entitled to claim anything for the same years . 

9 . I have already referred to the various notices issued to the union regardiag 
the hearing of this reference but the unioh has not appcared and has not Aled 
any statement and it appears that due to the settlement of the dispute pfore the 
Conciliation Officer the union and workmen are now satisfied and do not want to 
proceed with this demand . The copy of the memorandum of settlement belween 
the parties which has been produced by the employers along with their written 
statement supports the management that the parties had agreed to the payment of 
bonus equivalent to ten days wages per year in full and Anal settlement of the 
claim and it appears that after receiving the amount of 10 days wages as bonus 
the union again reiterated the same demand before the Conciliation Officer and I 
do not think that the same demand will now be justified. The copy of the settle 
ment shows that the settlement had taken place before the Conciliation Officer . 
It is signed both by the management and the representatives of the workers. It 
is also signed by the Conciliation Officer and there appears no reason why the same 
should not be accepted . In spite of the several notices the union has not remained 
present. Clearly the workmen are satisfied with the settlement and now they have no 
claim of any kind for the bonus for the two years 1962 -63 and 1963- 64 . It is also 
clear that the provisions of section 33 of the Payment of Bonus Act have been 
struck down by their Lordships of the Supreme Court in the case of Jalan 
Trading Co. Ltd ., V . Mill Mazdoor Sabha reported in 1966 II LLJ 546 . The 
workmen are not entitled to claim bonus for the two years under the Payment of 
Bonus Act and hence also the union has not taken part in the proceedings . The 
workmen employed in the stone quarry are not entitled to bonus for the two years 
1962 -63 and 1963 - 64 and hence my award accordingly . 
No order as to costs . 

Sd . ) - A . T . ZAMBRE, 

Presiding Oncer . 
Central Government Industrial Tribunal , Bombay .

AWARD 
By Order No. 28 ( 36 ) / 66 -LRIV dated 17th March 1966 the Government of India 
in the Ministry of Labour and Employment has referred to this Tribunal the 
industrial dispute between the employers in relation to Messrs . Darragh Smail and 
Company ( India ) Ltd ., Cochin - 3 and their workmen in respect of the matters spe 
cified in the following schedule 

SCHEDULE 
Whether the steamer tally clerks employed by M / s . Darragh Smail & Com 

pany ( India ) Ltd ., Cochin - 3 , are entitled for payment of bonus under 
the Payment of Bonus Act , 1965 for the accounting year ending on 31st 
March 1965 . If so , to what extent and to what other relief , if any, 

are they entitled ? " , 
2 . The management has by Its written statement opposed the claim and has 
contended that the steamer tally clerks were never the employees of the company . 
There was ny employer - employee relationship between them and they are not entit 
led to any bonus. They have denied that the working results of the company will 
enable bonus to be paid at 20 per cent and have contended that at any rate it 
should be considered on the basis of the accounts of the company which would 
be produced at the appropriate time. 

3. No statement of claim has been Alled on behalf of the workmen in this refer 
ence . 

4 . The reference was made to this Tribunal on 17th March 1966 and after the 
issue of notices and the Aling of statements the matter was adjourned from time 
to time. Ultimately in this sitting of the Tribunal at Ernakulam the parties nego 
tiated the dispute and settled it amicably and Aled a joint memorandum of setile 
ment under which the management has agreed to pay to the tally clerks bonus for 
the year ending on 31st March 1965 . The joint memorandum of settlement has 
been produced by the parties at annexure A . It is clear from the terms of settle 
ment that the tally clerks are to get bonus at the rate at which It was paid by the 

company to the head load labourers employed at Willingdon Island . The terms are 
reasonable and hence I pass an award in terms of the joint memorandum of settle 
ment annexure A which shall from part of this award . 

No order as to costs.

By Order No. 28 / 135 / 64 -LRIV datod 6th February 1965 the Government of 
India in the Ministry of Labour and Employment have referred to this tribunal 
the Industrial dispute existing between the employers in relation to Shri N . P . 
Nayak , Customs House Agent, Cochin 2 and their workmen in respect of the 
inatters specified in the following schedule : 

SCHEDULE 
" Whether the action of Shri N , P , Nayak , Customs House Agent, Cochin 2 

in refusing employment to Shri B . L . Mallja , as Tally Clerk with 
effect from 23rd November 1964 is justifled ? If not to what reliet is 

the workmen entitled ? " 
2 . The Steamer Tally Clerks Union which represents the workman Shri B . L . 
Mallia has by its statement to claim alleged that Shri Mallla was in the employ 
of Shri N . P . Nayak , Customs House Agent and was working regularly as a tally 
clerk for the last several years . As the management delayed payment of wages , 
Shri Mallia had to file a suit against the management for the delayed wages . This 
suit S . C . 268 of 1960 was compromised .

The union has alleged that though the suit was settld the management was 
waiting for an opportunity to victimise Shri Mallia and in the year 1964 they again 
began to cut his wages and delay the payments . Shri Mallia raised a complaint 
about it. However the inanagement in retaliation stoppal employing him from 
23rd November 1964 without assigning any reason . Hence Shri Mallia who was 
a member of the union approached the union who raised a dispute . It has beeii 
further alleged that Shri Mallia has been rcfused employment from 23rd November 
1964 and from that date till 29th March 1965 he would have been able to carn 
wages amounting to Rs. 282- 40 . Hence the union has prayed for a direction or 
his re- cirployment and claimed wages amounting to Rs. 282 :40 . 

3 . The company by its written statement has denied the claim of the workman 
and has contended that Shri Mallia was not employed in the seryice of the manage 
nient as such ant there was no employer - cmployec relationship between them . 
There was no industrial dispute within thic mcaning of the Industrial Disputes Act 
and Shri Mallia was not entitled to claini anything from ihem . They have ad

some tally but have contended that on cach of such occasions he was acting 
as an independent person . He used to submit his bills in respect of each such 
occasion and as cach separate tally work was a separate and independent piece of 
work at thc worst there was only an engagement of Shri Mallla by the manage 
ment on 21 contractual basis for finishing each item of work and the contractual 
relationship ceasca immediately the work was donc. This did not constitute Shri 
Mailla an employee of the management and there was no relationship of cinployer 
and conployee . I was not entitled to claim any loss of wages from the company. 

2 . After the receipt of this reference on 2nd March 1965 and the statements of 
the parties the matter was adjourned for hearing from time to time. But during 
this sitting of the Tribunal at Ernakulam the parties negotiated the dispute and 
have settled it amicably . They have filed a compromise petition at annexure A , 
According to the terms of the compromise the management has agreed to pay a 
sum of Rs. 200 to Shri Mallia in full and final payment of all the bills submitted 
by him and the claim for compensation made in this reference . The union by its 
statement of clalm hac demanded Rs. 282 -40 and considering the terms of compro 
mise it shall have to be held that the compromise is reasonable . I accept the same 
and pass an award in terms of tlie joint settlement annexure A which shall from 
part of this award . 


No order as to costs .

STATE ; Kerala . 

INDUSTRY : Major Ports and Docks . 
Bombay , the 30th April 1968 

AWARD 
The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour Employment and Rehabi 
litation (Department of Labour & Employment) have by their Order No. 28 ( 96 ) / 
466 -LRIV dated 15th September 1966 referred to this Tribunal an industrial dispute 
existing between the employers in relation to the Indo Ceylon Trading Company 
Ltd ., and their workmen in respect of the matters specified in the following sche 
dule :-- - 


SCHEDULE 
" Whether the Indo Ceylon Trading Company, Steamer Agents, Cochin - 9, are 

justified in refusing employment to Shri Donald Clements , Cargo , 
Supervisor on board the Country Crafts froin 14th March 1966 ? If 

not, to what relict is the workmen entitled ? " 
2 . The employers , the Indo- Ceylon Trading Co . Ltd ., are steamcr agents and 
commission agents with their office at Cochin . They are also agents for the Maldi 
vian Nationals Trading Corporation ( Ceylon ) Ltd ., and act as representatives of 
the Maldivian Government Trade Agents . The management had engaged the 
svorkmen Shri Donald as il cargo supervisor and he was in their employ he cuve 
him employment upto 14th March 1966 but subsequently denied employment to 
him and hence the Port and Steamer Workmen s Association of which he was a 
nember made representations against the management as a result of which this 
clispute was raised , 


2694 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8 , 1968 /JYAISTHA 18. 1890 


[PART II 


The Association has by its statement of claim contended that Shri Donald 
Clements was working with the management as a cargo supervisor for the steamers 
arriving at Cochin for the last nine yearg , His work was supervised and control 
led by the management and they used to pay him his remuneration at scheduled 
rates , Shri Donald Clements also worked with the management as a cargo super 
visor of the cargo loaded and unloaded at Cochin Port from the Maldivian country 
crafts arriving at Cochin Harbour and he did this work continuously for the last 
thrce years . He was efficient and dutiful but the management refused him emp 
loyment on the country craft S . V . Deefarum on 14th March 1966 and also subse 
quently without assigning any reason . When he as usual went to the office he 
was illegally denied employment. The management engaged their office clerk for 
the supervision work of the other countrycrafts which arrived at the Cochin Port 
and as Shri Donald Clements was illegally denied employment he was entitled to 
get his wages during the period and as the refusal of work was not justified Shri 
Donald Clements is entitled to reinstatement and the wages during the period he 
was refused employment. 


The company in its statement in reply has denied the claim of the Association . 
They have admitted that they had employed Shri Donald Clements as a super 
visor but have contended that he was engaged as and when the company required 
his services on payment of daily rate of wages. They have contended that they 
were not technically the agents for loading and unloading for the country crafts 
which called at Cochin and the company had to act as per the Tindal s (Master s ) 
requirements . The country crafts did not come under the purview of the Dock 
Labour Board for supply of labour . The country crafts make their own arrange 
ments by emploving ships crew or recruiting labour from shore . " The crafts 
mnentioned in the dispute advised the company that thcy did not require any 
lavour or tally or supervision work and thereforc the company did not arrange 
for labour and the services of Shri Donald were not required . As Shri Donald 
was not in the regular employ of the company there was no question of any 
lefusal of employment and he was not entitled to get any relief or wages . 


After the statements of claim an :l the reply and rejoinder were Aled by the 
partles the matter was adjourned from time to time, At the time of thls sitting 
of the Tribunal at Ernakulam the parlies negotiated the dispute and settled the 
some amicably and fled a memorandum of settlement at annexure A . By this 
settlement the inanagement has agreed to employ Shri Donald Clements on country 
crafts as before and has further agreed to pay him Rs. 76 / - towards his wages. 
The settlement is rcasonable and I accept the same and make an award in terms 
of the joint memorandum or set! lenient annexure A which shall form part of 
this award . 
No order as to costs .

" Whether the coal handling workers employed by M /s . Paul Abrao . and 

Sons, Cochin - 3 are entitled to the benefits given under the interim 
recommendations of the Central Wage Board for Port and Dock 

Workers ? If so to what extent and from what date ." 
The employers Messrs Paul Abrao and Sons are working as coal handling 
contractors of the Southern Railway at Willingdon Island , Cochin - 3 . The work 
men in their employment had raised a disputo through their union viz ., the Cochin 
Thuramugha Thozhilali Union for the interim relief recommendation by the Cen 
tral Wage Board for Port and Dock Workers and this dispute has been by the 
joint application of the employers and the workmen has been referred to this 
Tribunal for decision by the order dated 1st April, 1966 . 


After the receipt of reference notices dated 25th April 1988 , 14th February 
1968 were issued to the parties . In spite of these notices neither the employers nor 
the office bearers of the union Aled any statements of claim or reply . On the

Tribunal that the dispute between the workmen and the employers was compro

This is a complaint by one Shri C . Velu of Eraveli, Cochin - 1 against his 
employers Shri A . K . Muhamedkutty , Boat Owner and Contractor under seclion 
33A of thc Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 

2. The complainant bas alleged that he was employed by the opposite party 
as a tyndal for the last four years and was in their continuous employ since then 
The opposite party by their fetter dated 8th March , 1965 dismissed the petitioner 
from service alleging that he had committed certain misconduct . He has alleged 
that the management had not given him any opportunity to show cause against 
the charges. He was a workman concerned in industrial dispute Ref. No . CGIT 32 
of 1963. The employers also did not pay him one month s wages nor did they 
make any application for approval. The dismissal was illegal as the employers 
had contravened the provisions of section 33 of the Industrial Disputes Act , 1947 
and he should be reinstated . 

3 . After this complaint dated 27th June , 1965 notices were issued to the parties 
but the company did not Ale any written statement and the matter was adjourned 
from time to time. Even at the hearing fixed in Ernakulam on 4th March , 1968 
both parties were absent but Shri T . C . N . Menon , Advocate , who had filed the 
original complaint on behalf of the workmen informed the Tribunal that he had

heard that the workmen had compromisecl thc matter . However , again notices

AWARD 
The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour and Employment by their 
Order No. 28 139 84 LRIV dated 21st January 1965 have referred to this Tribunal 
an industrial dispute existing between the employers in relation to the Trustees 
of the Port of Bombay and their workmen in respect of the matters specified 
in the following Schedule : 

SCHEDULE 
" Whether the promotion of Shri D . D . Bendihale to the post of clectrician 

grade 1 on the MPV Venu in the place of Shri N . G . Patil is justified ? 
If not to what relief, if any , is Shri N . G . Patil entitled rd frorn 
what date ? " 


2 . The circumstances giving rise to the dispute may be stated in rei AS 
follows: 

The employers , the Trustees of the Port of Bombay had on or about the 7th 
of June 1962 commissioned the Motor Pilot Vessel Venu and put it into service . 
By their Resolution No. T . R . 569, dated 19th June, 1962 they had for this vessel 
sanctioned some staff including three electricians and three wiremen and hence 
the electric foreman of the Southern Division of the employers had issued a 


2698 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8 , 1968 / JYAISTHA 18 . 1890 


[ Part II 


notice inviting applications from the electricians and wiremen of both the electri 
cal sections for the post of electricians and wiremen in this vessel , At about 
that time the workman Shri D . D . Bendkhale who was holding the necessary 
qualification of first - class P . W . D . supervisor s certificate was working as electri 
cian grade II in the electrical establishment of the Bombay Port Trust and was 
posted at Wadala , while Shri N . G . Patil the other workman had only appeared 
for the examination for the necessary qualification and was working as a wire 
man in the electrical establishment in Southern Division . As per the notice Shri 
D . D . Bendkhale had submitted his application for the post on the pilot vessel 
through the head time- keeper Shri N . G . Patil had made two applications, 
There were other applications also and ultimately Shri N . G . Patil who passed 
the examination for the necessary qualification came to be appointed and was 
directed to report himself from 13th August, 1962 to the post of electrician on the 
MPV Venu . 

3 . It appears that in spite of applications of the candidates for the post of 
electrician grade I on the MPV Venu and the appointment of Shri N . G . Patil 
the result of the selection was not declared and Shri Bendkhale did not come to 
know that Shri Patil was selected for the post and that he had joined it . On 
oral enquiries Shri Bendkhale was told that the selection would be according 
to merits and seniority. He was working in the Northern Division of the Depart 
ment at Wadala and was completely in dark about the appointment. On the 27th 
September , 1963 when he was sent to the M . P . V . Venu in leave vacancy to work 
there as an electrician 1st grade he came to know about the selection and appoint 
ment of the junior person Shri N . G . Patil. In fact Shri Patil had passed the 
second class certificate examination for being appointed as electrician grade II 
only in the month of July , 1962 that is alter the last date for receiving the appli 
cations for the post of the electrician on the vessel VENU . On the date of appli 
cations he did not possess the requisite qualification for the post notified and 
henc.. Shri Bendkhale raised a clispute through the Employees Union and made 
Teresentations to the authorities about the injustice done to him . 

4 . The Chairman of the Bombay Port Trust thereafter made enquiries and came 
to a finding that Shri D . D . Benckhale who was originally appointed as electrician 
grade IJ. had for the post of electrician grade I on the MPV Venu a claim superior 
to Shri N . G . Patil who was only a wireman on the material date. The applica 
tion ci Shri Bendkhale had mysteriously disappeared . The Chairman therefore 
roverted Shri Patil and transferred him to the electrical establishment and 
appointed Shri D . D . Bendlchale as an electrician grade I on the MPV Venu . 
After this appointment Shri Patil made representations through the B . P . T , General 
Workers Union who raised this dispute. They had also approached the Concilia 
tion Officer and after the failure report it was referred to this Tribunol. 

5 . The B . P . T . General Workers Union representing the case of Shri N . G . 
Patll in whose place Shri D . D . Bendkhale was promoted have by their statement 
of claim contended that as per the notice inviting applications the right to apply 
for the post was not restricted only to electricians grade II but the appointments 
were thrown open to all employees of the Port Trust working as wireman as well 
X assistant wireman and the only requirement that the applicant should possess 
was the necessary qualification needed to work as electrician grade I. It is alleged 
that some four persons who had applied for the post and were selected refused 
to accept the transfer on the vessel VENU because of the service conditions and 
thereafter Shri Patil who had passed the examination held by the Maharashtra 
Government in July , 1962 was selected by the Deputy Conservator to work as 
electrician grude t and was appointed to do that work on the MPV VENU with 
effect from 14th August 1962. Thus Patil was appointed by the authorities through 
the process of selection and as he had been working on the MPV Venų with 
effect from 14th August, 1962 upto 29th June, 1964 he should have been considered 
permanent and confirmed against that post and his demotion was not justified . 

8 . As regards the claim made by Shri D . D . Bendkhalc the B . P . T . General 
Workers Union has alleged that there was no record available to show that Shri 
Benrikhale hail made an application for the post . He had also not raised any 
Quicction or made any protest against the appointment of Shri Patil and the sug 
gestion that his application was suppressed by somebody was far fetched . They 
have contended that it was only in the early part of 1964 that the B . P . T . 
Employees Union had demanded the promotion and transfer of Shri Bendkhale 
on M . P . V , VENU and approached the then General Manager of the Bombay Port 
Trust Shri Simoes on the subject. It is alleged that as Shri Simoes was favour 
ably inclined towards the B .PT. Employees Union he had ignored the requirem 
ments of law and had issued orders immediately for the reversion of Shri N . G . 
Patil and the promotion of Shri Bendkhale on the M . P . V . VENU . NO employee

working under the Chief Engineer , B . P . T ., in the electrical establishment had any

Bendkhale was not entitled to be promoted in place of Shri Patil and by promoting

There was absolutely no justification for the promotion of Shri Bendkhale and

7 . Shri D . D . Bendkhale is represented by the B . P . T . Employees Union who

gric: 94.61 employee can have no claim to the post. 


9 . It is contended that the fact of his having been a temporary or officiating 
enıployce is very clear from the monthly pay sheet as well as the wage slips issued 
to him under the Minimum Wages Act. Moreover Shri Patil had passed the first 
class wireman s certificate examination of the P . W . D . only in the month of July 
1982 that is after 18th June 1962 the last date for receiving the applications and 
he chini not even possess the requisite qualification on the relevant date . As re 
gourds the application of Shri Bendkhale it is submitted that he had put in his 
application on 11th June 1962 well within the last date for the receipt of the 
applications but it had mysteriously disappeared from the section of the head time 
keeper. The fact that he had put in his application for the said post was algo 
borne out by the statement dated 13th December , 1963 of Shri K , F . Rabadi, 
Churgoman . Electrical Establishinent. Northern Division , Wadala of the Chief 
Engineer s Department. Clearly he had made the application and he had a 
superio )" claim to the post of electrician on the M . P . V . VENU as against the claim 
of Slivi N . G .. Patil who was merely a wireman . 


10 . According to the employers Shri N . G . Patil who was a wireman was not 
eligible even to apply for the post of electrician on the M . P . V . VENU and the 
merc fact that the application of Shri Bendkhale had disappeared could not ex 
tinguish his superior claim for the appointment of electrician on the pilot vessel 
in preference to Shrt N , G . Patil. They have denied the allegations about 
favouritism to the B . P . T . Employees Union in the matter of promotion and have 
contender that Shri Patil was wrongly appointed as electrician on the pilot vessel 
as against the superior claim of Shri Bendkhale. It has been further contended 
that the appointments of Shri N . G . Patil and V . D . Taralkar were made on an 
ad hoc hasis as a stop gap arrangement to meet with the requirements and cannot 
be considered as permanent appointments or appointments made through the 
regular process of selection only at a later stage. The employers had realised that 


2703 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8 , 1968 / JYUISTHA 18 . 1890 


[PART IL 


Shri Bendkhale had a superior claim over that of Shri N , G . Patil and the General 
Manager had taken a just and proper decision in appointing Shri D . D : Bendkhale 
as electrician and in reverting Shri N . G . Patil ; that it was nelther uny unfair 
labour practice nor nepotism or favouritisni. On the contrary the injustice caused 
was undone and the promotion of Shri D . D . Bendkhale was justified . 

11. The reference pertains to a dispute regarding the promotion of an employee 
and so the Legal Adviser on behalf of the management has contended that as 
promotion is only a managerial function this Tribunal has no jurisdiction to deal 
with the same. He has relled upon the ruling reported in Brooke Bond case 1963 
ILLJ p . 250 and has argued that the dispute involves a pure question of promotion 
and it will not be open to this Tribunal to adjudicate upon it. I have already 
quoted the terms of the reference in the schedule by which this Tribunal has been 
asked to decide the question whether the promotion of Shri D . D . Bendkhale to the 
post of clectrician grade I on M . P . V . VENU in place of Shri N . G . Patil is justified 
and for deciding the same the Tribunal shall have to consider the comparative 
merits and other factors of the two candidates . However, I do not think that on 
that account only the matter will be taken out of the jurisdiction of the Tribunal. 
In the very ruling Brooke Bond case relled upon by the management the question 
involved was one of promotion and their Lordships have given the general formula 
jin respect of promotions in the following terms: 
" All things being equal seniority shall count for promotion . If the senior 

person has been overlooked in the question of promotion he is at 
llberty to ask the concern for reason why he has been overlooked in 

which case the concern shall give him the rcasons." 
In this ruling it has been further observed : 
" It is true that though promotions would normally be a part of the manage 

ment s function if it appears that in promoting one employee in pre 
ference to another the management has been actuated by malicious 
considerations or that the failure to promote one eligible person 
amounts to an unfair labour practice , that would be a different matter . 
But in the absence of mala fides normally it must be left to the dis 
cretion of the management to select which of the employees should 
be promoted at a given time subject of course to the formula to which 

we have just referred ." 
From these observations it is clear that though promotion is the function of the 
management the Tribunal can in certain cases transfer in the action taken by 
the management and I shall now discuss the evidence about the relative merlts 
of the candidates and whether there was any unfair labour practice or mala fides 
in the proniotion of Shri Bendkhale . 

12 . The employers had by their order transferred Shri N . G . Patil to the 
electrical department and had promoted Shri D . D . Bendkhale to the post of 
electrician grade I on the M . P . V . VENU after which the B . P . T . General Workers 
Union had made representations to the management on behalf of Shri N . G . Pauli 
contending that Shri N . G . Patil was appointed to the post of electrician grade I 
on the VENU with effect froin 14th August. 1962 . He was working against the 
permanent post on the vessel continuously and as the General Manager Shri 
Simoes was favourably inclined towards the B . P . T . Employees Union he ignored 
the requirements of the law and immediately issued orders for the reversion of 
Shri N . G . Patil and the promotion of Shri D . D . Bendkhale on the pilot vessel ; 
and the Bombay Port Trust was guilty of practising discrimination and encourag 
ing inter -union rivalries , and thereby provocating trade disputes . I have already 
observed that both Shri D . D . Bendkhalę and Shrị N . G . Patil were in the employ 
of the Port Trust and were working in the electric department. It is not in dis 
pute that the electric foreman had issued a notice inviting applications for the 
post and the last date for the receipt of applications was 18th June, 1962. It is 
also not in dispute that long before this notice Shri D . D . Bendkhale was working 
as electrician grade II and was holding the requisite qualification for the appoint 
ment 10 the post of electrician grade I. It is clear from the record that he had 
passed the examination for the first class certificate for P . W . D , in the year 1960 
and was qualified for the appointment while Shri N . G . Patil was working merely 
as a wireman on the day when the notice inviting the applications for the post 
was issued . At that time he had only appeared for the examination of the first 
class wireman s certificate Exhibit 5 which is the certificate produced by him 
dated 17th July , 1962 and it is clear that till the last date for submission of 

is 
applications for the post Shri Patil did not have even the necessary qualification 
for making an application and being appointed to the post . 


Sra . 3 (11) 


11 (, VELIF OL INDIT : JI VE 8 1968 / }} ISTILA 19 . 1894 


2700 


13 It is not in dispute that the channel of promotion in the engineering and 
port department is that an assistant wireman is to be promoted as wireman and 
tlie wireman is thereafter promoted as clectrician grade II who will be further 
cligible to be promoted as electrician grade I on the Pilot Vessel. It is clear from 
the extract exhibit E3 that the method of recruitment of electriciun on the M . P . V 
VENU is liv promotion of an electrician grade II on the basis of seniority - cum 
suitability It is further clear that only a person wlio holds a first class certificate 
PW . D . and is working as an electrician grade II is eligible for promotion to the 
post of clectrician on the pilot vessel Considering these requirements it is clear 
that Shirt D D . Bendkhale was eligible for promotion subject to other conditions 
regarding scniority and suitability . 

14 . However , the case of Shri N . G . Patil is quite different. I have already 
observed that he had 1101 the necessary qualifcation for appointnient to the post 
oſ electrician grade I. Not only he was not quallfiec! but as he was not working 
as electrician grade II he was also not eligible for that promotion . Shri D . D 
Bendkhali was working as electrician grade I. He was senior , qualified and 
cligible had it shall have to be held that Shri Bendkhale had far superior claims 
to the post in comparison to the chain of Shri N . G Patil. In fact considering 
the requi enient it shall have to be held that Shri Patil had absolutely no claim 
to the post . He was neither quailted for making the application nor was he 
eligible . He was not also senior and it is very surprising that the oficer concerned 
should l orward his name overriding the claims of others. 

15 Stiri S . Maitra the Gencral Secretary of the Bombay Port Trust General 
Workers Union has contended that according to the notiflcation the management 
had thrown upon the post to wiremen as well as electriciany grade Il . Shri Patil 
was a wireman and had made the application . He was selected and was also 
appointed to the post and had worked there about a year and a half and the sub 
sequent action of the management in reverting him and promoting Shri Bendkhale 
was not justified . I do not find any substance in the contention Considering 
the notice issued by the management it cannot be said that the post of the electri 
iian grado 100 the MPV VENU was thrown opcu even to wiremen , I have 
already in ntloned from the extracts exhibit E - 3 the method of recrultment to the 
post of clectrician grade I on the Pllot Vessel which shows that it is by promotion 
of the electrician grade II on the basis of seniority - cum - suitability . It cannot be 
read in the notice exhibit E - 1 that the post oť plccirician grade I on the vessel 
was thrown open to wirement. It is significant to remember that by the notice 
applications were invited for two categories of appointments - one for the post of 
electrician grade 1 and one for the post of wiremel) The notice reads: - 

Applications are invited for two posts of electricians grade I (Rs. 125 

6 - - 155 — EB — K — 185 ( C . C . scale ) equivalent Second Pay Cominission 
scale Rs. 175 — 6 — 205 — 7 — 240 and one post of wireman grade ( 1 ) Rs . 
76 — -5 — 105 , ( 2 ) 100 — 5 — 150 ( C & C scale ) equivalent Second Pay 
Commission Graus ( ) Rs. 125 — 3 — 131 - 4 - - 155 , ( 2 ) Rs 150 — 5 — 180 
for Pilot Vessel D . P . V . VENU belonging to the Deputy Conservator 
from elect irlans and wireman or assistant wirenien of both the 
clectrical sections possessing requisite cualifications." 


This notice shows that the assistunt wireman was to apply for the post of wireman 
and the cutention that even assistant wiremen could have applied for the post 
of electr : cia gracle I does not stand to reason , 


16 The contention that Shri Patil was appointed on regular selection atter the 
applications is not also supported by any record . The management has contended 
that it was an ad hoc appointment. It appears that there was neither any test 
nor ny interview and Shri Patil was transferred from the engineering department 
to the Purt Department. I have already observed that the last date for submitting 
applications as per the nuiice was 18th June , 1962 . It is not in dispute that Shri 
Patil 7211 not mci le any application for the post before this date . It is clear from 
exhibit E - 6 that lie Arst made an application on 19th July , 1962 and the second 
application on 26th July , 1962. His first application is for the post of clectrician 
2nd frarle ancl by the second application he had requested that he should be 
appointed as electrician on the M .PV. VENU . These applications cannot be said 
to have been made in the regular course as per the notifcation and it appears that 
alter the expiry of the date the olicor concerned took these applications from Shri 
Patil wind consilered his case with special favour. Neither the B .PT. General 
Workers Union nor Shri Patil has produced the order of his appointment to the 
post of clectrician grade I on MPV. VENU and it has been argued that no order 
was issued to him but only a memo was sent. The employers have produced the 


2702 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8 , 1968 /JYAISTHA 18 . 1890 


(Part II 


letter of the Mechanical Superintendent to the Deputy Conservator dated 11th 
August , 1982 exhibit E - 2 in which he has stated : 
" In continuation of my above I have to inform you that Shri Narayan 

Ganpat Patil wireman clectrical establishment S . D . who holds the 
necessary qualification of first - class P . W . D . supervisor s certificate pre 
scribed for the post of electrician is being directed to report to you 
from 13th August , 1962 for appointment in the vacant post of electri 
cian on the above vessel for which he has put in an application to 

the undersigned ." 
No appointment order had been issued to Shri Patil and there is no reason to dis 
belleve the management s contention that the appointment of Shri N . G . Patil 
was on an ad hoc basis by mere transfer and the contention of the Bombay Port 
Trust General Workers Union that he was regularly selected and was appointed 
to the permanent post cannot be accepted . 

17 . The B . P . T . Employees Union has contended that not only Shri D . D , 
Bendkhale was qualified for appointment to the post of electrician grade I on the 
M . P . V . VENU but he had also made an application in time. According to them 
his application was suppressed and the ocer favouring Shri Patil appointed him . 
It is clear that Shri Bendkhale made a representation and a complaint to the 
management in October 1963 when he was posted at the M . P . V . VENU in the 
leave vacancy . After this complaint the management had made enquiries about 
his original application and it is clear from the statement of Shri Rabadi dated 
13th December, 1963 that Shri Bendkhale had made an application . It is also 
clear that he had made a note about that application in the muster book , Shri 
Rabadi is a chargeman at Wadala , Shri Shetve for the B . P . T . Emaployees Union 
has contended that Shri Rabadi is a member of the B . P . T . General Workers Union 
and he would not tell a lle in favour of Shri Bendkhalę who was a member of the 
rival employees union . The statement of Shri Rabadi given in 1963 proves that 
Shri Bendkhale had made the application within time. Shri Bendkhale had the 
necessary qualification . He had made the application and it is clear that he was 
also entitled to get the post in preference to Shri Patil . 

18. It is true that Shri N . G . Patil worked at the post to which he was trans 
ferred from 14th August, 1962 to June 1963. However, this circumstance in itself 
will not entitle him to any claim to that post. I have already observed that no 
order of appointment has been issued in favour to Shri Patil. If we consider the 
terms and conditions of service stated in the notice it is clear that the appoint 
ment was to be on a temporary basis . In the notice itself It has been stated 
“ The selection will be made from candidates possessing requisite qualifica 

tions according to the seniority . 
The selected employees will be required to carry out their duties as per 

service condition shown below and as may be changed or required by 

the Dy . Conservator from time to time. 
The selected candidates will be appointed on a temporary basis in the Arst 

instance . They inay however be posted permanently if found guitable 
and will be transferred to the Dy. Conservator Department. On such 
transfer being effected guch employees will have no lien or claim for 

promotion to higher posts in the electrical section ." . 
Thus it is clear that the appointment of the selected candidate also would be on a 
temporary basis. Secondly : the post itself was of a temporary nature. I have 
alleady observed that the trustees of the Port of Bombay had commissioned the 
veggel M . P . V . Venu and had sanctioned some start on a provisional basis , The 
excerpts from the proceedings of the meeting of the trustees held on 19th June 
1962, exhibit E - 7 para 25 states: 
" It is expected that there will consequently be scope for an appreciable 

reduction in the crew of the M . P . V . Venu after sufficient experience 
has been gained on working it. Accordingly all staff proposals are 
provisional and are subject to review . .. . . " 


Thus it is clear that the post itself was temporary . The appointment was also 
to be made on a temporary basis and the contention of the B . P . T . General Workers 
Union that Shri Patil wus working against a permanent post does not stand to 
reason and in view of the temporary nature of the post Shri Patti cannot be 
said to have any claim over it . The learned Legal Adviser has invited any attention 
to the Supreme Court ruling reported in 1987 1 LLJ page 708 (712 ) . It has been 
held by this Court that when a first appointment or promotion is made on 


Sec. 3(1 )] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8 , 1968 / JYAISTHA 18 . 1890 


2703 


probation for a specific period and the employee is allowed to continue in the post 
after the expiry of the said period without any specific order of confirmation be 
continues as a probationer only and acqutres no substantive right to hold the post . 
In view of this ruling also it shall have to be held that Shri Patil had no claim 
of permanency over the post and the management had every right to transfer bim 
to the original department. 

19 . The General Secretary of the Bombay Port Trust General Workers Union 
has very seriously criticised the conduct of the management in promoting Shri 
Bendkhale and has alleged that the General Manager had practised discrimination 
and encouraged inter -union rlvarly . It has been argued that Shri Patil belonged 
to the B . P . T , General Workers Union while Shri Bendkhale was a member of the 
B . P . T . Employees Union and as the General Manager Shri Simoes was favourably 
inclined to the B . P . T . Employees Union he favoured Shri Bendkhale and promoted 
him to the post in place of Shri Patil. Shri Shetye the General Secretary of the 
B . P . T . Employees Union has contended that both Shri Patli and Shri Bendkhale 
were members of his union but after the representation of Shri Bendkhale against 
Shri Patil Shri Patil left the B . P . T . Employees Union and joined the B .P . T . General 
Workers Union and there was no question of encouragement of Interunion rivalry 
by the management. He has further argued that Shri Bendkhale had a superior 
claim to the post. Shri Patil was ineligible and unqualified and was appointed 
to the post even though he had not made any application In time. The management 
had merely undone the injustice that was caused to Shri Bendkhale because of the 
loss of his application and there was no question of any favouritism . In view of 
the circumstance that both Shri Bendkhale and Shri Patil formerly belonged to 
the B . P . T . Employees Union and that Shri Patil Joined the BPT. General 
Workers Union subsequently after the complaint by the Employees Union on 
behalf of Shri Bendkhale I do not And any substance in the grievance that the 
management was indulging in inter -union rivalry or was showing favouritiem , 
Had the management been favouring the B . P . T . Employees Union Shri Bendkbale 
would not have been required to suffer for such a long time in the whole affair . 

20 . I have already discussed the merits of the two candidates whose promotion 
is the subject matter of this refercnce. There is overwhelming evidence to show 
that Shri Bendkhale had superior claims to Shri Patil for the post of electrician 
grade I. on the M . P . V . Venu . The circumstances also clearly show that though 
Shri Patil did not cven make an application some officer who was interested in 
him had favoured him by taking the application and transferring him to the 
Deputy Conservator s Department. The claims of Shri D . D . Bendkhale were 
Ignored and by the subsequent order the management had undone the injustice 
caused to him . There is no question of any unfair labour practice or mala fides 
in the promotion of Shri Bendkhale and the action of the management in promoting 
Shri Bendkhale is justified . In view of this finding the question of any relief to 
Shri N , G . Pati does not arise . Hence my award accordingly . 


No order as to costs

is , an area whose insurable population was less than 500 ) in the district of Nadia 
in the State of West Bengal; 

And , whereas by virtue of its location in a sparse arca , the aforesaid factory 
was granted exemption from the payment of employers special contribution under 
section 73 - F of the Employees State Insurance Act, 1948 ( 34 of 1948 ) , until 
enforcement of the provisions of Chapter V of the Act in that area by the Central 
Government in the notification of the Government of India in the Ministry of 
Labour, Employment & Rehabilitation , ( Department of Labour and Employment) 
No . S . O . 3863 dated the 8th December , 1986 ; 

And, whereas the Central Government is satisfied that tlie insurable population 
of the Ranaghat area in the district of Nadia in the State of West Bengal has now 
exceeded 500 , and it is no longer a sparse area ; 

Now , therefore, in exercise, of the powers conferred by section 73 - F of the 
Employees State Insurance Act, 1948 ( 34 of 1948 ) , the Central Government here 
by makes the following further amendment in the said notification , namely : 
In the Schedule appended to the said notification , in the entries relating to 

Serial No . 6 , the entry " Ranaghat" in column 3 and the corresponding 
entries in column 4 shall be omitted , 

[No. 7 ,8 ( 38 ) /88 - HIIII.)

" 3 . A . Authority to determine the additional charges .- ( 1 ) In case any doubt or 

dispute arises as regards the additional charges leviable under clause 
3 , It shall be decided by the Excise Commissioner of the State where 

the particular distillery is located . 
( 2 ) Before taking the decision referred to in sub - clause ( 1 ) , the Excise Com 

missioner shall given adequate opportunity to the parties concerned to 

present their case .

tional charges , have power to call upon the seller and the buyer to

him for the purpose."

New Delhi, the 27th May 1968 
S . O . 2028. - The following draft rules further to amend the Curry Powder 
Grading and Marking Rules , 1950 , which the Central Government proposes to make 
in exercise of the powers conferred by section 3 of the Agricultural Produce (Grad 
ing and Marking ) Act, 1937 are published as required by the said section for the 
information of all persons likely to be affected thereby and notice is hereby given 
that the said draft will be taken into consideration on or after 1st July 1968 . 

Any objections and suggestions which may be received from any person with 
respect to the said draft before the date so specified will be considered by the 
Central Government. 

Draft Rules 
1 . These rules may be called the Curry Powder Grading and Marking (Amend 
ment) Rules , 1968. 

2 . In the Curry Powder Grading and Markļng Rules, 1956 , 
( a ) for rules 5 and 6 , the following rules shall be substituted , namely : 
" 5 . Grade Designation Marks. -- ( 1 ) The grade designation marks in the case 

of curry powder packed in ploythene or paper bags shall consist of 
a design incorporating the number of Certificate of Authorisation , 
the word Agmark and the grade approved by the Agricultural Mar 

keting Adviser . 
( 2 ) The grade designation mark in the case of curry powder packed in ting 

or glass containers shall consist of a paste - on label, specifying the 
grade designation and bearing the design of a map of India with the 

word Agmark . 
( 3 ) The grade designation mark in the case of curry powder packed in con 

tainers of Jute or cloth as also in containers in which sealed ploythene 
bags of graded curry powder are packed shall consist of a label, speci 
fying the grade designation and bearing the design ( consisting of an 
outline map of India with the word Agmark and the figure of the 
rising sun , with the words Produce of India and Flora " 

resembling the one as set out in Schedule I. 
6 . Method of Marking - - ( 1 ) The grade designation mark shall be securely 

affixed to or printed on each container in a manner approved by the 
Agricultural Marketing Adviser , 


ca. 8f11) ] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JUNE 8 , 1968 / JYAISTHA 18 . 1890 


2729 


( 2 ) In addition to the above , the following particulars shall also be clearly 

and Indelibly marked on each container namely : 
( a ) Date of packing in code or plain letters . 
(b ) Lot number, and 

( c ) Net weight. 
( 3 ) An authorised packer may , after obtaining the prior approval of the 

Agricultural Marketing Adviser , mark his private trade mark on a 
container , in a manner approved by the said officer , provided that the 
private trade mark does not represent a quality or grade different 
from that indicated by the grade designation mark affixed to or printed 

on the container in accordance with the rules ." 
( b ) In rule 7, for sub -rule (1 ) the following sub -rule shall be substituted , 
namely 
" ( 1 ) Only new and clean tins, bottles and polythene or jute or cloth con 

tainers or paper bags properly lined with suitable material shall be 
used for packing curry powder and these shall be closed and sealed 
in a manner approved by the Agricultural Marketing Adviser to the 
Government of India ."

(Department of Agriculture ) 

New Delhi, the 28th May 1968 
S . O . 2030 . - In exercise of the powers conferred by clause ( b ) of sub - section ( 2 ) 
of section 3 of the Essential Commodities Act, 1955 ( 10 of 1955 ) , the Central Gov . 
ernment hereby makes the following Order to amend the Arable Land ( Utilisation 
for Food Crops) Order, 1963, namely : 

1. This Order may be called the Arable Land (Utilisation for Food Corpe) 
Amendment Order , 1968. 

2 . In the Arable Land (Utilisation for Food Corps ) Orders, 1963 , for the proviso 
to sub - clause ( 1 ) of clause 3 , the following proviso shall be substituted , namely : 
" Provided that the Collector shall not issue such notice in respect of any land ; 
( a ) which is used and maintained as grass land and is classified as such 

in the revenue records; and 
( b ) which has been continuously used as grass land for a period of not less 

than four years Immediately preceding the date of notice , whether 

or not it was recorded as such in the revenue records; 
Provided further that the Collector shall not issue such notice in respect of 
any land which , under any law for the time being in force, is reserved in the year 
immediately proceeding the date of the notice for any of the folowing purposes , 
namely 

( a ) pasture land ; 
(b ) threashing floor ; 
( c ) Irrigation tank ; or 

( d ) any other public purpose . 
Explanation : - For the purposes of this clause " Grass land " means any land in 
the private ownership of an individual in which grass or fodder trees or both 
are grown .

" 9 . Payments to Cement Regulation Account. - -Every producer shall, in respect 
of each transaction by way of sale of cement ellected by him , pay within one 
month of the close of the month in which sales take place , to the Controller, an 
amount equivalent to the amount, if any, by which free on rail destination price 
of such cement released by him exceeds the aggregate of the following amounts , 
namely : 
(1 ) the ex - factory price of such cement calculated in accordance with the 

rates specified in the Schedule ; 
( 11 ) a selling agency commission calculated at the rate of Rs. 1 . 25 per tonne ; 
(iii ) the excise duty paid thereon; and

ment in respect of the packing or the containers under the Arst 

proviso to clause 8 :

एस० ओ० 2035 / मार०डी०पार०ए० /18जी/ 68 - - उद्योग (विकास तथा वियमन ) 
मधिनियम , 1951 ( 1951 का 65 ) की धारा 18छ और धारा 25 के द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों 
तथा इस सम्बन्ध में उसे समर्थ बनाने वाली अन्य सभी शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय 
सरकार एतद् द्वारा सीमेंट नियन्त्रण प्रादेश , 1967 में और आगे संशोधन करने के लिए 
निम्नलिखित आदेश जारी करती है :--- 


1 . इस आदेश को सीमेंट नियन्त्रण ( चौथा संशोधन ) आदेश , 1968 कहा जायगा । 
2. सीमेंट नियन्त्रण आदेश , 1967 में खण्ड 9 के स्थान पर निम्नलिखित रखा 

जाए, अर्थात् : 
9. सीमेट नियमन खाते में भुगतान 

प्रत्येक उत्पादक अपने द्वारा सीमेंट की बिक्री सम्बन्धी प्रत्येक सौदे के सम्बन्ध में 
ऐसे सीमेंट को जारी करने की तारीख से एक मास के अन्दर नियन्त्रक को उस राशि के 
बराबर राशि , यदि कोई हो , का भुगतान करेगा जो रेल द्वारा निशुल्क गतव्य स्थान के 
सीमेंट के मूल्य तथा निम्नलिखित राशियों से अधिक हो , अर्थात् : - - 

( 1 ) इस प्रकार के सीमेंट का कारखाने से चलते समय का मूल्य जो तालिका 

में निर्दिष्ट दरों के अनुसार निकाला गया हो ; 
( 2 ) बिक्री एजेंसी कमीशन जो 1. 25 रु० प्रति मीट्रिक टन की दर से निकन्ने

( 3 ) उस पर दिया गया उत्पादन शल्क ; और 
( 4 ) पैक किए हुए सीमेंट के मामले में खण्ड 8 के प्रथम परन्तुक के अन्तर्गत

का कोई अन्य साधन , जिसका केन्द्रीय सरकार द्वारा खण्ड 4 के अन्तर्गत उल्लेख किया गया हो

New Delhi, the 27th May 1968 
S . O . 2042 . — In exercise of the powers conſerred on me under sub -regulation 
(4 ) of regulation 3 of the Indian Standards Institution (Certification Marks ) 
Regulations, 1955 , as amended from time to time, and in partial modification of 
the notification published under number S . O , 1325 dated 30 March 1967, in the 
Gazette of India , Part II , Section 3 , Sub -section ( ii) dated 15 April 1967, it is , 
hereby , notifled that for purposes of the Indian Standards Institution Certifica 
tion Marks Scheme, IS : 779E - 1966 Emergency Indian standard specification for 
water meters ( domestic type) (third Emergency revision ) shall continue to 
remain in force as a normal standard after 10 January 1968 , namely the date of 
lifting the emergency by the Government of India .

(Department of Economic Affairs ) 

New Delhi, the 31st May 1968 
S . O . 2044 . — In exercise of the powers conferred by section 6 of the Indian Coin 
age Act, 1906 ( 3 of 1906 ) , the Central Government hereby makes the following 
amendment in the notification of the Government of India , in the Ministry of 
Finance (Department of Economic Affairs ) , No. S . Q . 1235 , dated the 29th March , 
1968, published in the Gazette of India dated the 6th April, 1903 , at Part II -Sec 
tion 3 sub - section (ii) , page 1771, namely : 

In the said notification , in the entries relating to 20 paise coin , under the head 
ing " No. of serrations" , for the entry " 114 " , the entry " 112 " shall be substituted .

CUSTOMS 


New Delhi, the 8th June 1968 


S . O . 2046. - In exercise of the powers conferred by sub - section ( 2 ) ot section 
79 read with sub - section ( 3 ) of section 160 of the Customs Act , ( 52 of 1962 ) , the 
Central Government hereby makes the following rules to further amend the 
Ceylon Baggage Rules published with the notification of the late Central Board 
of Revenue No . 31 - Customs, dated the 30th August, 1930 , namely : 


1. These rules may be called the Ceylon Baggage ( Amendment) Rules, 1968, 


2. In the Ceylon Baggage Rules, for rule 4 , the following rule shall be subs 
tituted , namely : 


" 4 . Notwithstanding anything to the contrary in rule ( 1 ) and subject to 

the exceptions specified in rule ( 2 ) , the unaccompanied baggage of 
a passenger may be passed free if imported within seven working 
days before or after his arrival : 


Provided that the proper officer may pags free any unaccompanied bag 

gage imported before or after the said period if he is satisfied that 
there are sufficlent grounds for not importing the said baggage with 
in the period aforesaid ." 


[ No. 86 / F .No. 10 /6 /68 - LC . II .) 


ORDER 


STAMPS 


New Delhi, the 1st June 1968 


3 . 0 . 2047,-- In exercise of the powers conferred by Clause ( a ) of sub -sectior 
( 1 ) of section 9 of the Indian Stamp Act, 1899 ( 2 of 1899 ) , the Central Government 
hereby remits the stamp duty with which the ad - hoc bonds to the value of sixty 
seven lakhs of rupees to be issued by the Haryana Financial Corporation , are 
chargeable under the said Act. 


[No. 8 / 88 — F . No. 1/ 28 /68- Cus.VII / Stamps. ) 


M S SUBRAMANYAM , Under Secy.

CENTRAL BOARD OF DIRECT TAXES 

INCOME - TAX 

New Delhi, the 22nd May 1968 
S . O . 2050. — In exercise of the powers conferred by sub - section ( 1 ) of section 122 
of the Income- tax Act , 1961 (43 of 1961) and of all other powers enabling it in that 
behalf, the Central Board of Direct Taxes hereby makes the following further 
amendments in the Schedule appended to its Notifcation No. 107 ( F . No . 50 / 5 / 86 
ITJ) , dated the 27th October , 1960 , namely : 

In the said Schedule against Rohtak Range under column 2 , the following 
entries shall be substituted , namely : 
Rohtak . 1 All Income-tax Circles , Wards or Districts having headquarters 

at:- 

( 1 ) Rohtak 
( ii) Gurgaon . 
( ill) Sonepat . 
( iv ) Hissar. 

( v ) Faridabad . 
2 . Special Survey Circle , Patiala ( in respect of persons who have 

their principal place of business in or reside in the Districts of 

Rohtak , Gurgaon and Hissar ) . 
This notification shall take effect from 23rd May, 1968 . 

Explanatory Note 
The amendment has become necessary on account of creation of a new Circle 
at Faridabad . 

( The above note does not form part of the notification but is intended to be 
merely clarificatory ) . 

[No. 41 ( F . No. 50 / 10 / 88 -ITJ) . 1

Where an Income- tax Circle , Ward or District or part thereot stands transferred 
by this Notification from one Range to another Range, appeals arising out cf 
assessments made in that Income-tax Circle , Ward or District or part thereof and 
pending immediately before the date of this Notifcation before the Appellate 
Assistant Commissioner of the Range from whom that Income Tax Circle , Ward 
and District or part thereof is transferred from the date this notification 
shall take effect , the transferred to and dealt with by the Appellate Assistant 
Commissioner of the Range to whom the said Circle , Ward or District or part 
thereof is transferred . 


Explanatory Note 
The amendments have become necessary on account of abolition of the charge 
of the Commissioner of Income-tax , Bihar and Orissa , Patna , and consequent 
creation of the charges of the Commissioner of Income-tax , Bihar, Patna and the 
Commissioner of Income- tax, Orissa , Bhubneshwar. 

( The above note does not forin part of the notification , but is intended to be 
merely clarifcatory ) .

red by this Notification from one Range to another Range, appeals arising out of 
assessments made in that Income- tax Circle , Ward or District or part thereof 
and pending immediately before the date of this notification before the Appellate

Madras, the 23rd May 1968 


S . O . 2054 . - In pursuance ofRules of the Central Excise Rules , 1944 , I empowerthe Assistant 
Collectors of Central Excise of this Collectorate , to exercise within their respective jurisdictions 
the powers of a Collector conferred by the provisions of the Rules enumerated in column 1 of the 
sub - joined Table , to the extent indicated in column 2 thereof. 


TABLE 


Central Excisc Rules 


Extent

MINISTRY OF EXTERNAL AFFAIRS 

New Delhi, the 1st June 1968 
s . o . 2060. --. The tollowing draft of Rules further to amend the Indian Pilgrim 

Ips Rules , 1933, as continued in force by clause ( a ) of sub-section ( 3 ) of section 
Rol of the Merchant Shipping Act , 1958 (44 of 1958 ), which the Central Govern 
ment proposes to make in exercise of the powers conferred by section 282 of the 
Sald Act, is hereby published, as required by the sald section 282 , for information 
of persons likely to be affected thereby , and notic Is hereby given that thesaid 
draft will be taken into consideration on or after the 7th July , 1968 . 

Any objection or suggestion , which may be received from any person with respect 
to the sand draft before the date so specified will be considerably by the Central 
Government. 

Draft Rules 
1. These rules may be called the Indian Pilgrim Ships ( Amendment ) Rules, 

1988. 
2. In the Indian Pilgrim Ships Rules, 1933 in sub- rule ( 2 ) of Rule 124 , for 

the words " A fee of ten rupees " the words " A fee of twelve rupees " 
shall be substituted . 

[ No. M II - 1 181 ( 19 ) / 67 ] 
T . T . P . ABDULLAH , 

Director 
(Personnel & Haj Affairs ) 


विदेश मन्त्रालय 


नं . दिल्ली, 1 जून 1968 
एस० नं . 2061. -च्यापार पोत अधिनियम , 1958 की धारा 282 द्वारा प्रदत्त अधि ारों का 
प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्र सरकार , निम्नलिखित नियम - प्रारूप से , भारतीय यात्री जहाज नियम 1933 
में , जो कि उक्त अधिनियम , ( 1958 का 44 ) की धारा 461 की उपधारा ( 3 ) खण्ड ( क ) द्वारा 
निरंतर लाग है और संशोधन करने का विचार कर रही है और ये नियम -प्रारूप , उन व्यक्तियों की 
सूचना के लिये जिन पर इसका प्रभाव पड सकता हा , इस अधिसूचना के माध्यम से प्रकाशित किए 
जा रहे है जैसा कि उक्त धारा 282 के अन्तर्गत करना होता है , और इसके द्वारा यह सचना दी 
जाती है कि इस प्रारूप पर 7 जुलाई 1968 को या उसके बाद विचार किया जाएगा । 

अगर इस प्रारूप के सिलसिले में उक्त तारीख से पहले किसी व्यक्ति से कोई आपत्तियां 
अथवा सझाव प्राप्त होंगे तो केन्द्र सरकार उनपर भी विचार करेगी ।
